Protein

P1

Lemon-Garlic Sheet-Pan Chicken Thighs

Makes 5 portions (about 136 g cooked each)Active 10 minTotal 40 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
280kcal
36 gprotein
2 gcarbs
13 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.60
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 60-90 sec, covered, until steaming hot (165 °F / 74 °C).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M1, A1, A8, D-1, D-8, C1, C7 · flavour families MEX, ASI, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 lb boneless skinless chicken thighs (907 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 3 tbsp lemon juice (45 g)
  • 4 cloves, minced garlic cloves (12 g)
  • 1 tsp dried oregano (1.5 g)
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika (2.5 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper (1 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C. Line a rimmed sheet pan with parchment.
  2. Whisk the oil, lemon juice, garlic, oregano, paprika, salt and pepper in a large bowl. Add the thighs and toss until every piece is coated. Leave 10 minutes, or up to 8 hours in the fridge.
  3. Spread the thighs in one layer with space between them. Do not stack.
  4. Roast 22-28 minutes. Done when the edges are browned, the juices run clear and a thermometer in the thickest piece reads 165 °F / 74 °C.
  5. Rest 5 minutes on the pan. Slice into strips or bite-size pieces.
  6. Portion into 5 containers (about 135 g each). Cool for up to 1 hour at room temperature, then refrigerate.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0. Goes in first, comes out at minute 25-28, then rests while you fill the oven with the next tray.

Fix it

  • Pale and wet instead of browned: the pan was crowded or the oven was cool. Put it back 5 minutes under the broiler, watching it closely.
  • Dry? Thighs forgive, but if they went past 185 °F, toss the slices with a spoon of the pan juices before storing.

Swap

Use bone-in thighs (about 2.75 lb, add 10 minutes) or chicken breast cut into 1-inch strips (cook 15-18 minutes). For no lemon, use 1 tbsp red wine vinegar.

Protein

P2

Slow-Cooker Salsa Shredded Chicken

Makes 5 portions (about 140 g cooked each)Active 10 minTotal 250 min
270kcal
46 gprotein
7 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.45
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 90 sec with 1 tbsp of its cooking juices, stir halfway, until steaming hot (165 °F / 74 °C).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M2, M9, M11, C8 · flavour families MEX, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2.2 lb boneless skinless chicken breasts (1000 g)
  • 1 1/2 cups (1 jar, 16 oz) jarred salsa (medium) (360 g)
  • 2 tsp ground cumin (4 g)
  • 2 tsp chili powder (5 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 2 tbsp lime juice (30 g)

Steps

  1. Lay the chicken breasts in the slow cooker in one layer if you can. Sprinkle with cumin, chili powder, garlic powder and salt.
  2. Pour the salsa over the top. Do not add water: the chicken gives off its own liquid.
  3. Cover. Cook on LOW 6-7 hours or HIGH 3-4 hours. Done when a thermometer in the thickest breast reads 165 °F / 74 °C and a fork shreds it with no effort.
  4. Move the chicken to a bowl. Shred with two forks or by beating it 20 seconds with a hand mixer.
  5. Stir in the lime juice and 1/2 cup of the cooking liquid. Taste and add a pinch of salt if needed.
  6. Portion into 5 containers (about 140 g each) with a spoonful of juices on each so it stays moist.

Sunday-sheet slot

Slow cooker, minute 0. It works alone for 3-4 hours on HIGH, so start it before anything else. It never touches the oven or the stove.

Fix it

  • Watery? Simmer the leftover liquid 5 minutes in a pan, or just use less when mixing back in.
  • Stringy and dry: it cooked too long on HIGH. Next time check at 3 hours, and always mix the juices back in.

Swap

Use boneless thighs (same weight, shred more tender) or a green salsa (tomatillo) for a milder, tangy version. Low on money: bone-in breasts, 3 lb, shredded after cooking.

Protein

P3

Never-Dry Baked Chicken Breast

Makes 5 portions (about 150 g cooked each)Active 10 minTotal 45 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
270kcal
45 gprotein
1 gcarbs
8 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.90
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave at 70% power for 60-90 sec, or slice and warm in a covered pan with a splash of water. Never full power for long.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes A5, A11, D-4, D-11, C4, C9 · flavour families MEX, ASI, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2.2 lb boneless skinless chicken breasts (1000 g)
  • 1 tbsp olive oil (14 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper (1 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika (2.5 g)

Steps

  1. Salt the chicken (the 1 tsp) on both sides 15-30 minutes before cooking, or overnight in the fridge. This is a dry brine.
  2. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C. Place a chicken breast between plastic wrap or in a bag and pound the thick end with a pan or rolling pin until it is an even 3/4 inch / 2 cm thick. Do all breasts.
  3. Rub with the oil, then the pepper, garlic powder and paprika. Lay on a lined sheet pan, with space between them.
  4. Bake 16-20 minutes. Start checking at 14. Done when a thermometer in the thickest part reads 160 °F / 71 °C, then it carries up to 165 °F / 74 °C as it rests.
  5. Rest covered with foil for 5-10 minutes. Cutting early lets the juices run out.
  6. Slice against the grain. Portion into 5 containers (150 g each) and drizzle any pan juices on top.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 25. Can share the oven with a veg tray at the same 425 °F / 220 °C.

Fix it

  • Rubbery or dry: it went past 165 °F or the breasts were uneven. Pound them flat next time and use the thermometer, not the clock.
  • White and wet on the surface: pat the chicken dry before the oil goes on.

Swap

Use thin-sliced breast cutlets (bake 10-12 minutes) to save pounding. Swap the spices for 2 tsp Italian seasoning or taco seasoning for a different family.

Protein

P4

Turkey Taco Crumble

Makes 5 portions (about 140 g cooked each)Active 20 minTotal 25 min
330kcal
35 gprotein
7 gcarbs
19 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.50
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 90 sec, stirring halfway, until steaming hot (165 °F / 74 °C).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M3, M10 · flavour families MEX

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 lb 93% lean ground turkey (907 g)
  • 1 medium, finely diced yellow onion (150 g)
  • 3 cloves, minced garlic cloves (9 g)
  • 1 tbsp olive oil (14 g)
  • 2 tbsp tomato paste (32 g)
  • 2 tbsp chili powder (16 g)
  • 2 tsp ground cumin (4 g)
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika (2.5 g)
  • 1 tsp dried oregano (1.5 g)
  • 1 1/2 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (9 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oil in a large skillet over medium heat. Add the onion and a pinch of salt. Cook 4-5 minutes until soft and see-through.
  2. Add the garlic, chili powder, cumin, paprika and oregano. Stir 30 seconds until it smells toasty.
  3. Add the turkey. Break it up with a wooden spoon or a potato masher. Cook 7-9 minutes until no pink is left and it reaches 165 °F / 74 °C (ground turkey is safe at 165 °F).
  4. Stir in the tomato paste, the salt and 1/2 cup water. Simmer 3-4 minutes until the liquid has mostly cooked off and the meat looks glossy, not soupy.
  5. Cool for 20 minutes, spread out in a wide dish. Portion into 5 containers (about 140 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop, one large skillet, minute 0 to 20. Frees the pan for a second batch of anything.

Fix it

  • Dry and crumbly: add 2-3 tbsp water and simmer 1 minute, then do the same when you reheat.
  • Bland: more salt and a squeeze of lime at reheat. Turkey needs more salt than beef.

Swap

Use 93% lean ground chicken (same weight) or ground beef (90%, adds fat and a little protein). Out of chili powder: 1 tbsp taco seasoning replaces the three spices.

Protein

P5

Beef & Lentil Power Mince

Makes 5 portions (about 160 g cooked each)Active 20 minTotal 50 min
330kcal
27 gprotein
27 gcarbs
13 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.10
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 90 sec, stirring halfway, until steaming hot (165 °F / 74 °C).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M4, M12, D-6, C5, C10 · flavour families MEX, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1 lb 90% lean ground beef (454 g)
  • 3/4 cup (150 g) dry brown lentils (150 g)
  • 1 medium, diced yellow onion (150 g)
  • 1 large, finely diced carrots (100 g)
  • 3 cloves, minced garlic cloves (9 g)
  • 1 tbsp olive oil (14 g)
  • 2 tbsp tomato paste (32 g)
  • 2 tsp ground cumin (4 g)
  • 2 tsp smoked paprika (5 g)
  • 1 tsp chili powder (2.7 g)
  • 1 1/2 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (9 g)

Steps

  1. Rinse the lentils in a sieve and pick out any stones.
  2. Heat the oil in a large pot over medium-high. Add the beef and break it up. Cook 6-8 minutes until browned and no pink is left (160 °F / 71 °C).
  3. Add the onion and carrot. Cook 5 minutes until the onion is soft.
  4. Add the garlic, tomato paste and spices. Stir 1 minute until the paste darkens a shade.
  5. Add the lentils, the salt and 3 cups water. Bring to a boil, then lower to a steady simmer, partly covered.
  6. Cook 22-28 minutes, stirring now and then. Done when the lentils are tender but not mushy and the pot is thick like chili, with almost no loose liquid. Add water 1/4 cup at a time if it dries too soon.
  7. Cool, then portion into 5 containers (about 160 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop, minute 0 to 45, one pot. Lentils simmer while you chop the rest. Lowest-cost protein of the book: 1 lb of beef covers 5 portions.

Fix it

  • Hard lentils after 30 minutes: old lentils. Add 1/2 cup water and simmer 10 more minutes.
  • Too wet: simmer uncovered 5 minutes. Too thick on day 3: loosen with a splash of water.

Swap

Use ground turkey in place of beef, or half the beef and a second 1/2 cup of lentils for the cheapest week. Red lentils cook in 12 minutes but go soft.

Protein

P6

Freezer Turkey Meatballs

Makes 5 portions (about 156 g cooked each)Active 15 minTotal 35 minOven 400 °F / 205 °C
360kcal
39 gprotein
10 gcarbs
18 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.45
Allergensmilk, egg, wheat
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 6 meatballs 60-90 sec, or 12 min at 350 °F / 175 °C from frozen, until 165 °F / 74 °C in the centre.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes A6, A10, D-5, D-12, C3, C12 · flavour families MED, COM, ASI

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 lb 93% lean ground turkey (907 g)
  • 1 large egg (50 g)
  • 1/2 cup plain dry breadcrumbs (55 g)
  • 1/4 cup (about 1 oz) grated Parmesan (28 g)
  • 3 cloves, minced garlic cloves (9 g)
  • 1 tsp onion powder (2.5 g)
  • 1 tsp dried oregano (1.5 g)
  • 2 tbsp, chopped fresh parsley (8 g)
  • 1 1/2 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (9 g)
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper (1 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 400 °F / 205 °C. Line a rimmed sheet pan with parchment.
  2. In a large bowl, mix the egg, breadcrumbs, Parmesan, garlic, onion powder, oregano, parsley, salt and pepper. Let it sit 3 minutes so the crumbs soak up the egg.
  3. Add the turkey. Mix with your hands only until it just comes together. Overmixing makes them tough.
  4. Scoop 30 even balls (about 2 tbsp each, a bit over 1 oz / 35 g) and roll them smooth. Wet your hands if it sticks.
  5. Place on the pan, close but not touching. Bake 18-20 minutes. Done when browned on top and a thermometer in the middle reads 165 °F / 74 °C.
  6. Cool 15 minutes. Portion 6 meatballs per container (about 155 g). To freeze, lay on a tray for 1 hour, then bag.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 25. Freezer champion: double the batch and freeze the second half flat on a tray, then bag.

Fix it

  • Dry meatballs: you overmixed, or baked past 165 °F. Reheat them in a covered pan with a spoonful of sauce.
  • Falling apart: the mix was too wet. Add 1 more tbsp breadcrumbs and chill 10 minutes.

Swap

Gluten-free: swap the breadcrumbs for 1/2 cup rolled oats pulsed fine. Dairy-free: leave out the Parmesan and add 1/4 tsp more salt. Ground chicken works too.

Protein

P7

Oven Pulled Pork Shoulder

Makes 5 portions (about 156 g cooked each)Active 15 minTotal 300 minOven 300 °F / 150 °C
430kcal
54 gprotein
5 gcarbs
20 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.30
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 90 sec with 1 tbsp of its juices until steaming hot (165 °F / 74 °C).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M5, A3, A12, C2, C11 · flavour families MEX, COM, ASI

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 3 lb, after trimming (buy about 3 3/4 lb) trimmed boneless pork shoulder (butt) (1361 g)
  • 1 tbsp brown sugar (13 g)
  • 1 tbsp smoked paprika (7 g)
  • 2 tsp ground cumin (4 g)
  • 2 tsp garlic powder (6 g)
  • 2 1/2 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (15 g)
  • 1 tsp black pepper (2 g)
  • 1/4 cup apple cider vinegar (58 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 300 °F / 150 °C. Trim off the thick fat cap and any big fat seams you can reach with a knife (the macros here are for trimmed meat). Mix the sugar, paprika, cumin, garlic powder, salt and pepper. Rub it all over the pork.
  2. Place the pork in a Dutch oven or a deep baking dish. Pour in the vinegar and 1/2 cup water around it, not over it.
  3. Cover tightly with a lid or double foil. Roast 4 to 4 1/2 hours.
  4. Done when a thermometer reads 195-205 °F / 90-96 °C and a fork twists in with no resistance. Safe at 145 °F, but it only shreds once the connective tissue melts at the higher temperature.
  5. Rest 20 minutes, covered. Move to a board. Discard any bone and leftover fat. Shred with two forks.
  6. Skim the fat from the pan juices. Toss the pork with 1/2 cup of the juices. Portion into 5 containers (about 155 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 270. Start it first. At 300 °F it blocks the oven for 4-4.5 hours, so P7 is a slow-Sunday protein: pair it with stovetop bases and cold veg, or cook it Saturday and shred on Sunday.

Fix it

  • Pork will not shred: it needs more time. Re-cover and give it another 30-45 minutes.
  • Dry after reheating: always store with its juices; add 1 tbsp water to the container before the microwave.

Swap

Same recipe in a slow cooker: LOW 8-10 hours, no extra liquid except the vinegar. Leaner and faster: use pork tenderloin (cook to 145 °F), but it will not be as juicy.

Protein

P8

Simple Baked Salmon

Makes 5 portions (about 146 g cooked each)Active 8 minTotal 22 minOven 400 °F / 205 °C
410kcal
37 gprotein
1 gcarbs
27 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $4.50
Allergensfish
Fridge3 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatEat cold, or microwave at 50% power 60-90 sec. Do not boil it; it dries and smells.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M8, A4, A9, D-3, D-10, C6 · flavour families MEX, ASI, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 lb (skin-off or skin-on) Atlantic salmon fillets (907 g)
  • 1 tbsp olive oil (14 g)
  • 1 tbsp lemon juice (15 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika (2.5 g)
  • 3/4 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (4.5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp black pepper (0.5 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 400 °F / 205 °C. Line a sheet pan with parchment.
  2. Pat the salmon dry. Cut into 5 even portions (about 180 g raw each).
  3. Place on the pan, skin side down. Rub with the oil, lemon juice, garlic powder, paprika, salt and pepper.
  4. Bake 11-14 minutes. Done when the thickest part flakes with a fork and reads 145 °F / 63 °C. It should look opaque with a slight shine inside, not dry and chalky.
  5. Rest 3 minutes. Cool for up to 1 hour at room temperature, then refrigerate in a sealed container.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 15. Quick tray: put it in last if the oven is busy, but eat it on days 1-2 of the week for best quality.

Fix it

  • White liquid on top (albumin): you cooked it hot and fast. Lower to 375 °F and shorten the time next batch.
  • Smells fishy on day 4: it is past its best. Plan this protein for the first 2 days and freeze the rest the same day.

Swap

Use arctic char or steelhead trout (same weight). For a budget version, use canned salmon (14.75 oz cans) mixed with lemon, but the macros change.

Protein

P9

Crispy Baked Tofu

Makes 5 portions (about 136 g cooked each)Active 15 minTotal 55 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
340kcal
32 gprotein
11 gcarbs
21 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.30
Allergenssoy, wheat
Fridge4 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatOven or air fryer, 400 °F / 205 °C for 6-8 minutes to bring back the crisp. Microwave makes it soft.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M6, A2, A7, D-7 · flavour families ASI, MEX, MED

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 blocks (16 oz / 454 g each), extra-firm extra-firm tofu (908 g)
  • 3 tbsp cornstarch (24 g)
  • 3 tbsp soy sauce (48 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika (2.5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper (1 g)

Steps

  1. Drain the tofu. Wrap in clean towels, set a heavy pan on top and press 20 minutes.
  2. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C. Line a sheet pan with parchment.
  3. Tear or cut the tofu into 3/4-inch / 2 cm cubes. Toss gently with the soy sauce and oil, then the garlic powder, paprika and pepper.
  4. Sprinkle on the cornstarch and toss until the pieces look dry and lightly coated, with no white clumps.
  5. Spread in one layer with space between pieces. Bake 15 minutes, flip, bake 15-18 minutes more.
  6. Done when the outside is golden and firm, and a cube sounds hollow when you tap it. It crisps more as it cools. Portion into 5 containers (about 135 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 35 (after a 20-minute press that happens while you prep everything else).

Fix it

  • Soft, not crispy: not pressed enough, or the pan was crowded. Pat dry harder and use two pans.
  • Stuck to the pan: the parchment was missing or you flipped too soon. Wait until the edge lifts easily.

Swap

Gluten-free: use tamari (check the label) instead of soy sauce. Use baked tempeh cubes for more protein per portion, or pressed cubes of extra-firm tofu with 1 tbsp more oil in an air fryer.

P10

Smoky Spiced Chickpeas

Makes 5 portions (about 140 g cooked each)Active 8 minTotal 35 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
260kcal
11 gprotein
36 gcarbs
9 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.60
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatMicrowave 60 sec, or 5 minutes in a hot oven or air fryer to bring back the edges.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M7, D-2, D-9 · flavour families MED, MEX

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 3 cans (15 oz), drained and rinsed canned chickpeas, drained and rinsed (750 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 2 tsp smoked paprika (5 g)
  • 1 tsp ground cumin (2 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 1 tbsp lemon juice (15 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C.
  2. Drain and rinse the chickpeas. Spread on a towel and pat very dry. Dry beans roast. Wet beans steam.
  3. Toss on a sheet pan with the oil, paprika, cumin, garlic powder and salt.
  4. Roast 25-30 minutes, shaking the pan once at the halfway point. Done when the chickpeas are golden, shrunken a little and the skins split.
  5. Toss with the lemon juice right out of the oven.
  6. Cool, then portion into 5 containers (about 140 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 30. Cheapest protein in the book, and the only plant protein P with no pressing or soaking.

Fix it

  • Soft in the fridge: normal, they take on moisture. Re-crisp them 5 minutes in the oven or air fryer.
  • Burnt spice on the pan: the oven was too hot or the pan was dark. Lower to 400 °F / 205 °C and use parchment.

Swap

Use white beans or black beans (canned, rinsed). Skip the smoke with 2 tsp Italian seasoning for a Mediterranean version. Each portion has about 11 g of protein, about a third of the chicken recipes: pair it with a higher-protein sauce like Greek yogurt.

Chapter 13

Bases

Rice, quinoa, potatoes, sweet potatoes, pasta and tortillas. The base is the part that usually ruins day 4: hard rice, mushy pasta, soggy potatoes. Each card is written to stop that.

How to use this block
  • Rice and quinoa: cool them fast and spread thin (Chapter 4). Reheat with 1 Tbsp of water, covered.
  • Pasta: cook it 1-2 minutes short, toss with oil. It finishes cooking when you reheat it.
  • Potatoes: reheat in the oven or air fryer. The microwave makes them soft. Potatoes don't freeze well; sweet potatoes do.
  • Tortillas: no cooking. Store them in a bag and roll the wrap at lunch, so it isn't soggy.

B1

Hands-Off Oven Rice

Makes 5 portions (about 160 g cooked each)Active 5 minTotal 45 minOven 375 °F / 190 °C
230kcal
4 gprotein
44 gcarbs
3 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.15
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatMicrowave 90 sec with 1 tbsp of water, covered, until steaming hot (165 °F / 74 °C).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M1, M2, M5, M8, M10, A1, A3, A6, A7, A9, D-1, D-6, D-10, C4, C11 · flavour families MEX, ASI, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1 1/2 cups long-grain white rice (278 g)
  • 1 tbsp olive oil (14 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 375 °F / 190 °C. Boil 2 1/4 cups of water in a kettle or pot.
  2. Rinse the rice in a sieve under cold water for 30 seconds. Drain well.
  3. Put the rice, oil and salt in an 8x8-inch / 20 cm baking dish (or any dish with a tight lid). Pour in the boiling water and stir once.
  4. Cover tightly with foil, crimping all the edges. Bake 25-30 minutes without opening it.
  5. Done when all the water is absorbed and small steam holes show on the surface. Leave covered 10 minutes, then fluff with a fork.
  6. Spread the rice out in a thin layer to cool, then portion into 5 containers (160 g each) within 1 hour of cooking. Refrigerate right away.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 30, then rest 10 minutes off. Fits next to a 425 °F tray if you cover it well; or cook it before the high-heat trays.

Fix it

  • Crunchy grains: the foil leaked steam. Add 2 tbsp hot water, re-cover and give it 10 minutes.
  • Gummy: too much water or no rinse. Spread on a pan and dry 5 minutes in the oven.

Swap

Brown rice: use 1 1/2 cups rice and 2 3/4 cups boiling water, bake 55-60 minutes. Cauliflower rice is not a swap in this recipe (it does not bake like rice).

Base

B2

Fluffy Quinoa

Makes 5 portions (about 168 g cooked each)Active 5 minTotal 25 min
210kcal
7 gprotein
33 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.50
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 60-90 sec with a splash of water, covered, until steaming hot (165 °F / 74 °C).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M6, A2, A4, A12, D-2, D-3, D-5, D-7 · flavour families ASI, MED, MEX, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1 1/2 cups quinoa (255 g)
  • 1 tbsp olive oil (14 g)
  • 3/4 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (4.5 g)

Steps

  1. Rinse the quinoa in a fine sieve for 30 seconds, rubbing it with your hand. This removes the bitter coating.
  2. Add the quinoa, 3 cups water, oil and salt to a medium pot. Bring to a boil.
  3. Lower to the lowest heat, cover and cook 14-16 minutes. Done when the water is gone and you can see the little white spiral tails on each seed.
  4. Take off the heat, keep the lid on and leave 5 minutes. Fluff with a fork.
  5. Spread on a tray to cool 15 minutes. Portion into 5 containers (about 165 g each) and refrigerate within 1 hour.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop, minute 0 to 20, one pot with a lid. Hands free after the first 5 minutes.

Fix it

  • Mushy: too much water or you lifted the lid. Next time stick to 2 cups of water per 1 cup of quinoa.
  • Bitter: it was not rinsed enough.

Swap

Use millet or couscous in the same weight if quinoa is too pricey. Cheaper: half quinoa, half rice, cooked separately.

B3

Crispy Roasted Baby Potatoes

Makes 5 portions (about 170 g cooked each)Active 10 minTotal 45 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
190kcal
4 gprotein
33 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.50
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatOven or air fryer, 400 °F / 205 °C for 8-10 minutes. Microwave makes them soft.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M9, M12, D-11, C1, C5, C6 · flavour families MEX, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2.2 lb baby red potatoes, halved (1000 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika (2.5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper (1 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C. Put the empty sheet pan in the oven to heat.
  2. Halve the potatoes (quarter any bigger than a golf ball). Dry them with a towel.
  3. Toss in a bowl with the oil, salt, garlic powder, paprika and pepper.
  4. Carefully tip onto the hot pan, cut sides down. They should sizzle.
  5. Roast 20 minutes, flip, then roast 15-20 minutes more. Done when the cut sides are deep golden and a knife slides in with no push.
  6. Cool, then portion into 5 containers (about 170 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 40 (long tray, so put it in first). Goes on the lower rack at 425 °F / 220 °C.

Fix it

  • Soggy: they were wet or the pan was crowded. Dry them and use two pans.
  • Hard centres, burnt edges: pieces were uneven. Cut to the same size, and give the big ones 10 minutes extra.

Swap

Use Yukon Gold potatoes cut into 1-inch chunks. Not good for the freezer: they turn grainy and watery.

Roasted Sweet Potato Cubes

Makes 5 portions (about 170 g cooked each)Active 15 minTotal 45 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
220kcal
3 gprotein
41 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.60
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 60-90 sec, covered, or 8 minutes in a 400 °F / 205 °C oven to re-crisp.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M3, A5, A11, D-8, C2, C12 · flavour families MEX, ASI, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2.2 lb (about 3 medium), peeled, 3/4-inch cubes sweet potatoes (1000 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika (2.5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper (1 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C. Line a pan with parchment.
  2. Peel the sweet potatoes and cut into 3/4-inch / 2 cm cubes. Keep them the same size so they cook evenly.
  3. Toss with the oil, salt, paprika and pepper. Spread in one layer, not touching.
  4. Roast 15 minutes, flip, then roast 12-15 minutes more.
  5. Done when the edges are browned and caramelised, and a fork slides into the centre with no resistance.
  6. Cool, then portion into 5 containers (about 170 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 35. Needs one flip. Works on the same rack as other 425 °F trays.

Fix it

  • Soft and no browning: the pan was crowded. Use two pans or reduce to a single layer.
  • Burnt edges, hard middle: cubes too large. Next time cut to 3/4 inch.

Swap

Butternut squash cubes work in the same time. For a sweeter finish, add 1/2 tsp cinnamon in place of the paprika.

B5

Reheat-Ready Pasta

Makes 5 portions (about 150 g cooked each)Active 5 minTotal 20 min
280kcal
9 gprotein
51 gcarbs
4 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.30
Allergenswheat
Fridge4 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatMicrowave 60-90 sec with 1 tbsp of water or sauce, covered, until steaming hot (165 °F / 74 °C).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes A8, A10, C3, C7, C10 · flavour families COM, ASI, MED

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 12 oz (340 g) penne or fusilli short dry pasta (penne or fusilli) (340 g)
  • 1 tbsp olive oil (14 g)
  • 1 tbsp (for the water; only part is absorbed) kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)

Steps

  1. Bring 3 quarts / 3 L of water to a rolling boil. Add the salt.
  2. Add the pasta. Cook 1-2 minutes LESS than the package says. It must still have a firm centre, since it cooks more when you reheat it.
  3. Check by biting a piece: you should see a thin white dot in the centre.
  4. Drain. Do not rinse. Toss right away with the oil so the pieces do not stick together.
  5. Spread on a tray to cool 15 minutes. Portion into 5 containers (about 150 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop, minute 0 to 15 while the oven trays roast. Boil the water first thing so it is ready.

Fix it

  • Clumped together in the fridge: not enough oil. Add a splash of water on reheat and break up with a fork.
  • Mushy after reheating: it was overcooked to start with. Pull it 2 minutes early next time.

Swap

Gluten-free: use a corn-and-rice pasta and cook 2 minutes less. Higher protein: use chickpea or lentil pasta (this changes the macros, recalculate).

B6

Wraps & Tortillas (Store & Assemble)

Makes 5 portions (about 50 g cooked each)Active 5 minTotal 5 min
150kcal
4 gprotein
25 gcarbs
4 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.35
Allergenswheat
Fridge7 days (opened package)
Freezer2 months
ReheatWarm 15 seconds per side in a dry pan, or 10 seconds in the microwave wrapped in a damp towel. Assemble the wrap at lunch.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M4, M7, M11, D-4, D-9, D-12, C8, C9 · flavour families MEX, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 5 flour tortillas, 8-inch flour tortillas (8-inch) (250 g)

Steps

  1. Check the date on the package. Keep the tortillas in the fridge once opened.
  2. Stack the tortillas in two groups, with a square of parchment between each.
  3. Slide them into a zip bag and press out the air. For 5 days, one bag is enough.
  4. To freeze beyond a week: wrap in foil, then a freezer bag. Defrost overnight in the fridge.
  5. Assemble the wrap on the day. Warm the tortilla, then fill with 1 protein portion, a vegetable portion and a spoonful of sauce, and fold in the sides before rolling.
  6. For a lunch you take with you: pack the filling in a container and the tortilla in a bag, and assemble at lunch to avoid sogginess.

Sunday-sheet slot

No cooking at all. Minute 0: portion and wrap. Do this while the oven works.

Fix it

  • Cracks when you fold it: it is cold or dry. Warm 15 seconds in a dry pan to make it bendable.
  • Soggy by lunch: the filling was wet. Keep sauce in its own cup and add it at the last minute.

Swap

Use corn tortillas (6-inch, 2 per portion) for gluten-free tacos, or low-carb wraps, but check the label. Check macros for any wrap that is not plain flour.

Chapter 14

Vegetables

Four roasted, two raw. Roasted vegetables cook hot and fast so they don't turn to mush by Thursday. Raw ones are cut and dressed separately so they stay crisp.

How to use this block
  • Space on the pan is the whole trick. One layer, pieces not touching. Crowded vegetables steam and go limp. Use a second pan.
  • Broccoli roasts hottest (450 °F). Do it first on Sunday, then lower the oven.
  • Zucchini (V3) and the cucumber-tomato salad (V6) keep 3 days, not 4. Plan them for Monday-Wednesday.
  • Dressings stay apart until you eat (V5, V6).
  • Most vegetables don't freeze well. The fajita peppers (V2) are the exception.

V1

Charred Roasted Broccoli

Makes 5 portions (about 130 g cooked each)Active 10 minTotal 30 minOven 450 °F / 230 °C
110kcal
5 gprotein
12 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.30
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatEat cold in bowls, or 10 minutes in a hot oven or air fryer. Microwave makes it soft but it is safe.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M10, M12, A1, A5, A7, A9, A12, D-7, D-8, D-11, C2, C6, C7 · flavour families MEX, ASI, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 large crowns (about 2 lb), cut into florets broccoli crowns (900 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 450 °F / 230 °C.
  2. Cut the broccoli into bite-size florets, with a flat cut side if possible. Cut thick stems into coins.
  3. Dry well. Toss with the oil, salt and garlic powder.
  4. Spread over a large pan with space between pieces. Place cut sides down.
  5. Roast 18-22 minutes without stirring more than once. Done when the tips are blackened in spots, the stems pierce with a knife, and the florets look dark green and slightly shrunken.
  6. Portion into 5 containers (about 130 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 22 at 450 °F / 230 °C. Hottest tray of the day. Do it first, then lower the oven for the rest.

Fix it

  • Limp and olive-colored: too wet, too crowded or too low an oven. Dry it and use 2 pans.
  • Bitter, burnt tips: oven too hot or pan too close to the top. Lower the rack.

Swap

Use cauliflower (same time) or Brussels sprouts halved (add 5 minutes). Frozen broccoli: roast from frozen at 450 °F for 25 minutes, less crisp.

V2

Sheet-Pan Fajita Peppers & Onions

Makes 5 portions (about 130 g cooked each)Active 10 minTotal 35 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
110kcal
2 gprotein
14 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.70
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 60 sec, or a hot dry pan for 3 minutes for better texture.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M1, M3, M6, M11, C5, C8 · flavour families MEX

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 3 bell peppers (red, green or mix), sliced bell peppers (red and green) (600 g)
  • 2 medium, sliced in 1/2-inch strips yellow onion (300 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 2 tsp chili powder (5 g)
  • 1 tsp ground cumin (2 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 1 tbsp lime juice (15 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C.
  2. Slice the peppers and onions into strips about 1/2 inch / 1 cm wide, all the same size.
  3. Toss with the oil, chili powder, cumin, garlic powder and salt.
  4. Spread over a large pan in one layer. Do not crowd.
  5. Roast 25-28 minutes, stirring once at 15 minutes. Done when the onions are soft and golden at the edges and the peppers have blistered spots.
  6. Squeeze the lime juice on top. Portion into 5 containers (about 130 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 28 at 425 °F / 220 °C. Shares the oven with P1, P3, P9 and P10.

Fix it

  • Watery: the pan was too crowded. Use 2 pans or drain the liquid before storing.
  • Too crunchy: slice them thinner next time and leave 5 more minutes.

Swap

Add 1 sliced poblano or a jalapeno for heat. If peppers are expensive, use half peppers and half zucchini.

V3

Roasted Zucchini & Red Onion

Makes 5 portions (about 130 g cooked each)Active 10 minTotal 30 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
90kcal
2 gprotein
9 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.75
Allergensnone
Fridge3 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatEat cold, or microwave 45 sec. Best on the first 3 days; it gets watery after.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M4, M9, D-2, D-3, D-9, C3, C9, C10 · flavour families MEX, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 3 medium (about 1.7 lb), cut in 1/2-inch half-moons zucchini (750 g)
  • 1 large red onion, cut in wedges (200 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 1 tsp dried oregano (1.5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp garlic powder (1.5 g)
  • 1 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (6 g)
  • 1 tbsp lemon juice (15 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C.
  2. Cut the zucchini in 1/2-inch half-moons. Cut the onion into thick wedges, keeping the root so the layers hold together.
  3. Toss with the oil, oregano, garlic powder and salt.
  4. Spread over a large pan in one layer. Zucchini likes space and high heat.
  5. Roast 20-25 minutes, turning once at 12 minutes. Done when the edges are golden brown and the middle is tender, not soft and mushy.
  6. Add the lemon juice. Portion into 5 containers (about 130 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 25 at 425 °F / 220 °C. Zucchini goes in last in the week-1 order, as it turns watery the longest in the fridge.

Fix it

  • Mushy and wet: pan crowded or pieces too thin. Next time keep them thick and use 2 pans.
  • Watery by day 3: normal. Pat dry with a paper towel before eating.

Swap

Use yellow squash, or eggplant cubes (add 5 minutes). Add 1/2 tsp of smoked paprika for a stronger taste.

Vegetable

V4

Garlic Green Beans

Makes 5 portions (about 120 g cooked each)Active 10 minTotal 25 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
100kcal
3 gprotein
11 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.95
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatEat cold in bowls, or microwave 45-60 sec.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes A4, A6, A10, A11, D-5, C1, C4, C11, C12 · flavour families ASI, MED, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1.5 lb, trimmed green beans (700 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 4 cloves, minced garlic cloves (12 g)
  • 3/4 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (4.5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp black pepper (0.5 g)
  • 1 tbsp lemon juice (15 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C.
  2. Trim the ends off the green beans. Dry them well.
  3. Toss with the oil, salt and pepper. Spread in one layer on a large pan.
  4. Roast 10-12 minutes until they start to wrinkle.
  5. Take out, scatter the garlic over, stir, and roast 4-6 minutes more. Done when the beans are wrinkled with brown spots and still have a little snap, and the garlic is pale gold, not dark.
  6. Add the lemon juice. Portion into 5 containers (about 120 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, minute 0 to 18 at 425 °F / 220 °C. The garlic goes on in the last 5 minutes.

Fix it

  • Bitter garlic: it burned. Always add it late, and take it out when pale gold.
  • Limp beans: roasted too long or too low. Next time use the top rack and check at 12 minutes.

Swap

Use asparagus (cook 8-10 minutes) or snap peas (7-9 minutes). Frozen green beans work: roast from frozen for 20 minutes.

Vegetable

V5

Sesame Cabbage-Carrot Slaw (raw)

Makes 5 portions (about 130 g cooked each)Active 15 minTotal 15 min
110kcal
2 gprotein
13 gcarbs
7 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.50
Allergenssesame, soy, wheat
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatNot reheated. Eat cold. Keep the dressing in a jar and toss on the day for crunch.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M5, M8, A2, A3, A8 · flavour families ASI, MEX, COM

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1/2 small head (about 1 lb), thinly shredded green cabbage (450 g)
  • 2 large, grated carrots (150 g)
  • 3, thinly sliced scallions (40 g)
  • 1 tbsp toasted sesame oil (14 g)
  • 1 tbsp canola oil (14 g)
  • 3 tbsp apple cider vinegar (45 g)
  • 1 tbsp soy sauce (16 g)
  • 1 tbsp honey (21 g)
  • 1 tbsp toasted sesame seeds (9 g)
  • 1/2 tsp ground ginger (1 g)

Steps

  1. Shred the cabbage as thin as you can with a sharp knife. Grate the carrots. Slice the scallions.
  2. In a jar or bowl, whisk the sesame oil, canola oil, vinegar, soy sauce, honey, ginger and half the sesame seeds until the honey dissolves.
  3. Combine the vegetables in a big bowl. Keep the dressing apart for now.
  4. Portion the vegetables into 5 containers (about 130 g each) with a small cup or section for the dressing (about 1 1/2 tbsp each).
  5. When eating, pour the dressing over and toss. Top with the rest of the sesame seeds.
  6. Taste the dressing: it should be tangy and a bit sweet. Add a pinch of salt or 1 tsp more honey to taste.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold prep, no heat. Do it while the oven runs. Dress it only on days 1-2 if you want maximum crunch.

Fix it

  • Watery slaw: it was dressed too early. Keep the dressing separate and drain any liquid.
  • Too sharp: add 1 tsp honey or 1 tbsp oil.

Swap

Allergy to sesame: leave out the seeds and use 1 tbsp more canola oil. Gluten-free: use tamari. Use pre-shredded coleslaw mix to save 8 minutes.

Vegetable

V6

Chopped Cucumber-Tomato Salad (raw)

Makes 5 portions (about 180 g cooked each)Active 15 minTotal 15 min
80kcal
2 gprotein
8 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.60
Allergensnone
Fridge3 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatNot reheated. Eat cold. Add the dressing on the day.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: signature dishes M2, M7, D-1, D-4, D-6, D-10, D-12 · flavour families MED, MEX

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 medium, diced cucumbers (400 g)
  • 1 lb Roma or cherry tomatoes, diced tomatoes (Roma or cherry) (400 g)
  • 1/2 small, finely diced red onion (80 g)
  • 1/4 cup, chopped fresh parsley (15 g)
  • 2 tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 2 tbsp lemon juice (30 g)
  • 1/2 tsp dried oregano (0.8 g)
  • 3/4 tsp kosher salt (table-salt equivalent) (4.5 g)

Steps

  1. Dice the cucumbers and tomatoes into 1/2-inch pieces. Scoop out the seedy centre of the tomatoes if they are very juicy.
  2. Finely dice the red onion. Soak it in cold water for 5 minutes, then drain, to take away the sharp bite.
  3. Mix the cucumber, tomato, onion and parsley in a bowl. Do not add the salt yet.
  4. In a jar, shake the oil, lemon juice, oregano and salt until creamy.
  5. Portion the salad into 5 containers (about 180 g each) lined with a paper towel, with the dressing in a small cup.
  6. On eating day, pour the dressing over. It should taste bright and a little salty, with crisp cucumber.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold prep, no heat. Prep it last, as it is the most perishable thing in the week.

Fix it

  • Soggy: salt and dressing draw out water. Dress only at the last minute.
  • Bland tomatoes: add a pinch of sugar and extra lemon. Cherry tomatoes hold up better than big ones.

Swap

Add 1/2 cup crumbled feta or a can of chickpeas for a heartier salad (macros change). Use bell pepper if cucumbers are out of season.

Chapter 15

Sauces · The Variety Engine

"I noticed you reuse a lot of ingredients, seasonings, and sauces like the hot honey."

— reader review

Same chicken, three sauces, three different meals. That's how one Sunday gives you five plates that don't taste like the same box. There are 12 sauces, 3 per flavor family. Most take 5-15 minutes and one small saucepan, or no heat at all.

How to use this block
  • Pick 3 sauces a week, ideally from 2-3 families.
  • Store each sauce in its own cup. It goes on after reheating. Sauce stored on rice or pasta makes it soggy.
  • Yogurt sauces (S1, S7, S11) are never heated and don't freeze. Everything else freezes for 2-3 months: make a double batch and freeze half flat in a bag.
  • Sauces are where the allergens hide: soy sauce (soy, usually wheat), tahini (sesame), peanut butter (peanuts), Worcestershire (fish), yogurt (milk). Check the column.

Sauce

S1

Chipotle-Lime Yogurt

Makes 5 portions of sauce (40 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 5 min
30kcal
4 gprotein
2 gcarbs
1 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.35
Allergensmilk
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatServe cold. Don't heat it. Stir before using.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Macro note: Chipotle in adobo is costed with the closest USDA entry (canned hot red chili peppers); brands vary a little.

Goes with: M1, M3, M6, M8, M10, M12 (all the Mexican plates with S1)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 3/4 cup plain nonfat Greek yogurt (170 g)
  • 1 pepper canned chipotle pepper in adobo, minced (+ 1 tsp of the adobo sauce) (15 g)
  • 1 Tbsp lime juice (15 g)
  • 1 small clove garlic, grated (3 g)
  • 1/4 tsp ground cumin (0.5 g)
  • 1 tsp olive oil (4.5 g)
  • 1/8 tsp fine salt (0.7 g)

Steps

  1. Mince the chipotle pepper as fine as you can. Grate the garlic.
  2. Spoon the yogurt into a bowl or a 1-cup jar.
  3. Add the chipotle, 1 tsp adobo sauce, lime juice, garlic, cumin, oil and salt. Stir until the color is an even pale orange with no white streaks.
  4. Taste. Add a pinch more salt if it tastes flat, or a little more adobo if you want heat.
  5. Scoop into one small lidded container or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 40 g each). Chill at least 30 min so the garlic and chipotle settle in.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. Make it in the 5-minute gap while the oven preheats or the chicken rests. No burner, no oven.

Fix it

  • Too thin? Your yogurt was watery. Stir in 1 more Tbsp of yogurt, or drain it in a sieve for 10 min next time.
  • Too hot the next day? Heat builds as it sits. Stir in 1 Tbsp yogurt to calm it.

Swap

No dairy: use unsweetened plain coconut or soy yogurt (macros change). Less heat: use half the chipotle.

Sauce

S2

Tomatillo Salsa Verde

Makes 5 portions of sauce (45 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 10 minTotal 20 minOven broil, high
20kcal
0 gprotein
3 gcarbs
0 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.25
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatEat cold or at room temperature. For a warm sauce, simmer 3 min in a small pan.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: M2, M5, M7, M9 (Mexican plates with S2)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 3-4 medium tomatillos, husked and rinsed (170 g)
  • 1 jalapeño, stem removed (15 g)
  • about 1/4 small white onion, quartered (30 g)
  • 1 clove garlic, in the skin (3 g)
  • 1/4 cup packed fresh cilantro, leaves and tender stems (8 g)
  • 1 tsp lime juice (5 g)
  • 1/3 tsp fine salt (2 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the broiler on high with a rack 4-6 inches below the heat. Line a small sheet pan with foil.
  2. Put the tomatillos, jalapeño, onion and unpeeled garlic on the pan. Broil 5-7 min until the tops are blistered and spotted black.
  3. Flip everything. Broil 4-5 min more until the tomatillos are soft, slumped and leaking juice. The garlic should feel soft when squeezed.
  4. Cool 5 min. Slip the garlic out of its skin. Tip everything, juices too, into a blender with cilantro, lime juice and salt.
  5. Pulse 8-10 times for a bit of texture, or blend longer for smooth. Taste and add salt until it tastes bright, not flat.
  6. Pour into a lidded jar or 5 mini containers (about 3 Tbsp / 45 g each). Cool fully before closing.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven (broiler) for 12 min. Fits in the last 20 minutes while the main tray rests, or right after the main tray comes out.

Fix it

  • Too sour or sharp? Tomatillos vary. Stir in 1/4 tsp honey or sugar.
  • Too watery? Simmer in a pan 4-5 min, stirring, until it coats the back of a spoon.

Swap

Mild: take the seeds and ribs out of the jalapeño. No broiler: pan-roast in a dry cast-iron skillet over medium-high heat, turning, 10 min. Not hot enough? Add a second jalapeño.

Sauce

S3

Quick Red Enchilada Sauce

Makes 5 portions of sauce (40 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 15 min
30kcal
1 gprotein
3 gcarbs
2 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.20
Allergenswheat
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatWarm in a small pan or microwave 30-45 sec until steaming.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: M4, M11 (Mexican plates with S3)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 tsp olive oil (9 g)
  • 2 tsp all-purpose flour (5 g)
  • 1 Tbsp chili powder (8 g)
  • 1/4 tsp ground cumin (0.5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp garlic powder (0.8 g)
  • 1/4 tsp dried oregano (0.3 g)
  • 1/2 cup tomato sauce (plain, canned) (122 g)
  • 1/3 cup water (80 g)
  • 1 tsp cider vinegar (5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp fine salt (3 g)

Steps

  1. Warm the oil in a small saucepan over medium heat until it shimmers, about 1 min.
  2. Whisk in the flour, chili powder, cumin, garlic powder and oregano. Cook 45-60 sec, whisking, until it smells toasty and looks like wet sand. Don't let it go dark.
  3. Pour in the tomato sauce, water and vinegar while whisking. Scrape up any paste from the bottom.
  4. Add the salt. Simmer on low 8-10 min, stirring now and then, until it coats the back of a spoon and the color deepens to brick red.
  5. Taste. Add a pinch of salt if it tastes flat. Cool, then spoon into one jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 42 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Fire. One burner, 15 min, low and unattended after minute 5. Fits while the oven is heating.

Fix it

  • Bitter? The spices burned. Stir in 1/2 tsp honey and 1 tsp tomato sauce; if still bitter, start again with lower heat.
  • Too thick when cold? Thin with 1-2 tsp water when you reheat.

Swap

Gluten-free: use 1 tsp cornstarch instead of the flour. Hotter: add 1/8 tsp cayenne.

Sauce

S4

Lighter Teriyaki

Makes 5 portions of sauce (35 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 12 min
40kcal
1 gprotein
7 gcarbs
0 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.20
Allergenssoy, wheat, sesame
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatWarm in a pan or microwave 30 sec. Stir; it thickens as it cools.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: A1, A4, A10 (Asian plates with S4)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1/4 cup reduced-sodium soy sauce (60 g)
  • 1/4 cup water (60 g)
  • 1 1/2 Tbsp honey (32 g)
  • 1 Tbsp rice vinegar or cider vinegar (15 g)
  • 1 tsp fresh ginger, finely grated (2 g)
  • 1 clove garlic, grated (3 g)
  • 1/2 tsp toasted sesame oil (2 g)
  • 2 tsp cornstarch (5 g)
  • 1 Tbsp (for the cornstarch) water (15 g)

Steps

  1. Whisk the cornstarch with 1 Tbsp cold water in a small cup until smooth. Set aside.
  2. In a small saucepan, combine soy sauce, 1/4 cup water, honey, vinegar, ginger and garlic. Bring to a gentle simmer over medium heat, stirring.
  3. Give the cornstarch cup another stir, then pour it in while whisking. Simmer 1-2 min until the sauce turns glossy and coats the back of a spoon.
  4. Remove from the heat and stir in the sesame oil.
  5. Cool 10 min. Pour into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 34 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Fire. One burner, 10 min. Easy to run while the oven is on.

Fix it

  • Lumpy? The cornstarch wasn't mixed smooth. Pour through a small sieve.
  • Too thin? Simmer 1 more min. Too thick when cold? Whisk in 1 tsp warm water.

Swap

Gluten-free and soy-free: use coconut aminos and the same amount of honey, and taste for salt. Less sweet: use 1 Tbsp honey.

Sauce

S5

Peanut-Ginger Sauce

Makes 5 portions of sauce (35 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 5 min
100kcal
3 gprotein
7 gcarbs
7 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.15
Allergenspeanuts, soy, wheat, sesame
Fridge4 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatServe cool or room temperature. Thin with 1 tsp warm water if it has set firm.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: A2, A5, A8, A12 (Asian plates with S5)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1/4 cup smooth peanut butter (no sugar added) (65 g)
  • 1 Tbsp reduced-sodium soy sauce (16 g)
  • 1 Tbsp lime juice (15 g)
  • 1 Tbsp honey (21 g)
  • 1 tsp fresh ginger, finely grated (2 g)
  • 1 clove garlic, grated (3 g)
  • 1/2 tsp toasted sesame oil (2 g)
  • 1/2 tsp sriracha (3 g)
  • warm, 1/4 cup water (60 g)

Steps

  1. Put the peanut butter in a bowl. Add soy sauce, lime juice, honey, ginger, garlic, sesame oil and sriracha.
  2. Whisk it. It will look thick and grainy at first. Keep going.
  3. Whisk in the warm water 1 Tbsp at a time. Stop when it falls from the whisk in a smooth ribbon.
  4. Taste. More lime if it's heavy, more soy sauce if it's flat.
  5. Spoon into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 37 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 5 minutes anywhere in the session. No heat.

Fix it

  • Split or oily? Your peanut butter was cold or natural-style with a layer of oil. Whisk in 1 Tbsp warm water.
  • Too thick after chilling? Whisk in 1 tsp warm water per portion when you serve it.

Swap

Peanut allergy: use sunflower seed butter (nut-free; macros change). Spicier: double the sriracha.

Sauce

S6

Gochujang-Honey Sauce

Makes 5 portions of sauce (30 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 10 min
30kcal
1 gprotein
6 gcarbs
1 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.25
Allergenssoy, wheat, sesame
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatServe cool or warm 20 sec in the microwave. It thickens in the fridge; stir in a splash of water.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Macro note: USDA has no gochujang entry. It is costed with the closest entry (sriracha chili sauce), which likely UNDERSTATES calories by roughly 10-15 kcal per portion. Check your jar's label.

Goes with: A3, A6, A7, A9, A11 (Asian plates with S6)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 3 Tbsp gochujang (Korean chili paste) (50 g)
  • 1 Tbsp honey (21 g)
  • 1 Tbsp reduced-sodium soy sauce (16 g)
  • 1 Tbsp rice vinegar or cider vinegar (15 g)
  • 1 tsp toasted sesame oil (4.5 g)
  • 2 cloves garlic, grated (6 g)
  • 1 tsp fresh ginger, finely grated (2 g)
  • 3 Tbsp water (45 g)

Steps

  1. In a small saucepan, whisk the gochujang, honey, soy sauce, vinegar, garlic, ginger and water until smooth.
  2. Heat over medium-low, stirring, until small bubbles break at the edges, about 2-3 min. Do not boil hard; it spits.
  3. Simmer 1 min until it looks glossy and slightly thicker. Take it off the heat and stir in the sesame oil.
  4. Cool 10 min. Spoon into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2 Tbsp / 30 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Fire. One small burner, 8 min. Fits any gap.

Fix it

  • Too spicy? Stir in 1 tsp more honey and 1 tsp water.
  • Too salty? Gochujang brands differ. Add 1 tsp water and 1 tsp vinegar next time, and use less soy sauce.

Swap

Gluten-free: check the gochujang label (many brands contain wheat) and use tamari. Milder: swap 1 Tbsp of gochujang for tomato paste.

Sauce

S7

Tzatziki

Makes 5 portions of sauce (45 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 8 minTotal 8 min
40kcal
3 gprotein
2 gcarbs
2 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.30
Allergensmilk
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatServe cold. Don't heat. A little water on top is normal; stir it back in.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: D-1, D-4, D-9, D-10, D-12 (Mediterranean plates with S7)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2/3 cup plain nonfat Greek yogurt (150 g)
  • 1/4 cup cucumber, grated and squeezed dry (40 g)
  • 2 tsp lemon juice (10 g)
  • 2 tsp olive oil (9 g)
  • 1 small clove garlic, grated (3 g)
  • 1/2 tsp dried dill (0.5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp fine salt (1.5 g)

Steps

  1. Grate the cucumber on the big holes. Wrap it in a clean towel or paper towel and squeeze hard until only a damp clump is left.
  2. Stir the yogurt, lemon juice, oil, grated garlic, dill and salt in a bowl until smooth.
  3. Fold in the cucumber.
  4. Taste. Add salt until the cucumber tastes sweet, not watery. Spoon into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 43 g each). Chill 30 min.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 8 minutes any time. Do it first so the flavors blend.

Fix it

  • Watery next day? The cucumber wasn't squeezed enough. Spoon off the liquid and stir in 1 Tbsp thick yogurt.
  • Garlic too sharp? Let it sit 1 hour, or use half a clove.

Swap

No dairy: unsweetened plain soy or coconut yogurt. Fresh dill: use 1 Tbsp chopped in place of dried.

Sauce

S8

Lemon-Tahini Sauce

Makes 5 portions of sauce (35 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 5 min
70kcal
2 gprotein
3 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.30
Allergenssesame
Fridge4 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatServe cool. It sets firm in the fridge; whisk in 1 Tbsp water per portion.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: D-2, D-6, D-7, D-8, D-11 (Mediterranean plates with S8)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1/4 cup tahini (stir the jar first) (60 g)
  • 3 Tbsp lemon juice (45 g)
  • cold, 1/4 cup water (60 g)
  • 1 small clove garlic, grated (3 g)
  • 1/4 tsp fine salt (1.5 g)
  • a pinch ground cumin (0.3 g)

Steps

  1. Put the tahini in a bowl. Add the lemon juice, garlic, salt and cumin. Whisk. It will seize into a thick, pale paste. That's normal.
  2. Whisk in the cold water 1 Tbsp at a time. The paste loosens and turns creamy and light.
  3. Stop when it falls from the whisk in a thick ribbon.
  4. Taste. More lemon if it's heavy, more salt if it's flat. Spoon into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 34 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 5 minutes anywhere. No heat.

Fix it

  • Bitter? Cheaper tahini can be. Add 1 tsp honey and 1 tsp lemon.
  • Grainy or split? Whisk in 1 tsp cold water at a time until smooth again.

Swap

Sesame allergy: use sunflower seed butter (macros change). Sweeter: add 1 tsp honey.

Sauce

S9

Chimichurri

Makes 5 portions of sauce (40 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 10 minTotal 10 min
140kcal
0 gprotein
2 gcarbs
15 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.35
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatThaw in the fridge overnight, or at room temperature 20 min only, then stir.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: D-3, D-5 (Mediterranean plates with S9)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1 cup packed fresh flat-leaf parsley, leaves and tender stems (60 g)
  • 3 cloves garlic (9 g)
  • 3 Tbsp red wine vinegar (45 g)
  • 1/3 cup olive oil (73 g)
  • 1 tsp dried oregano (1 g)
  • 1/4 tsp red pepper flakes (0.5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp fine salt (3 g)

Steps

  1. Wash the parsley and dry it well. Wet leaves make a watery sauce.
  2. Chop the parsley and garlic fine by hand, or pulse 5-6 times in a food processor until it looks like coarse crumbs. Don't purée it.
  3. Tip into a bowl with the vinegar, oregano, pepper flakes and salt. Stir and let sit 5 min.
  4. Stir in the oil. Taste. It should be sharp and green. Add salt if it tastes flat.
  5. Spoon into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 38 g each). Press a thin film of oil over the top. Refrigerate right away.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 10 minutes. Make it last so it's freshest.

Fix it

  • Dull brown-green after a day? That's normal. A squeeze of lemon on serving brings it back.
  • Too sharp? Add 1 Tbsp more oil, or reduce the vinegar next time.

Swap

Nothing fresh? Use 1 cup packed cilantro for a different but good version. Milder: use 1 garlic clove. Always refrigerate; fresh garlic in oil must not sit at room temperature.

Sauce

S10

Smoky BBQ Sauce (Less Sugar)

Makes 5 portions of sauce (40 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 20 min
40kcal
1 gprotein
10 gcarbs
0 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.20
Allergensfish
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatWarm in a pan or microwave 30 sec. Stir.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: C1, C2, C5, C8, C11 (Comfort plates with S10)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1/3 cup ketchup (85 g)
  • 2 Tbsp tomato paste (32 g)
  • 2 Tbsp cider vinegar (30 g)
  • 1 Tbsp brown sugar (14 g)
  • 1 tsp yellow mustard (5 g)
  • 1 tsp Worcestershire sauce (5 g)
  • 1 tsp smoked paprika (2.3 g)
  • 1/2 tsp garlic powder (1.5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp onion powder (1.2 g)
  • a pinch cayenne (0.2 g)
  • 1/4 tsp fine salt (1.5 g)
  • 1/4 cup water (60 g)

Steps

  1. Whisk everything in a small saucepan: ketchup, tomato paste, vinegar, brown sugar, mustard, Worcestershire, paprika, garlic powder, onion powder, cayenne, salt and water.
  2. Bring to a gentle bubble over medium heat, then turn to low. Simmer 10-12 min, stirring every few minutes.
  3. It's done when a spoon leaves a clear trail across the bottom of the pan and the sauce looks dark and glossy.
  4. Taste. More cayenne for heat, more vinegar for tang. Cool 10 min.
  5. Spoon into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 40 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Fire. One burner, 20 min, mostly unattended on low. Start it first.

Fix it

  • Splattering? Turn the heat down and half-cover the pan.
  • Too tangy? Stir in 1 tsp honey. Too thick when cold? Whisk in 1 tsp water.

Swap

No fish allergen: use 1 tsp soy sauce in place of Worcestershire (adds soy and wheat). Lower sugar still: use 2 tsp brown sugar.

Sauce

S11

Honey-Mustard Yogurt

Makes 5 portions of sauce (45 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 5 min
40kcal
3 gprotein
7 gcarbs
0 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.35
Allergensmilk
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatServe cold. Don't heat.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Macro note: Costed with yellow mustard entry; Dijon is similar.

Goes with: C4, C6, C9, C12 (Comfort plates with S11)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2/3 cup plain nonfat Greek yogurt (150 g)
  • 2 Tbsp Dijon or yellow mustard (30 g)
  • 1 1/2 Tbsp honey (32 g)
  • 1 tsp lemon juice (5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp garlic powder (0.8 g)
  • 1/4 tsp fine salt (1.5 g)
  • a pinch black pepper (0.3 g)

Steps

  1. Put the yogurt in a bowl.
  2. Add the mustard, honey, lemon juice, garlic powder, salt and pepper.
  3. Stir until smooth and pale gold, with no streaks of mustard or honey.
  4. Taste. More honey if it's sharp, more mustard if it's sweet.
  5. Spoon into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 44 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 5 minutes anywhere. No heat.

Fix it

  • Too thin? Strain the yogurt next time, or stir in 1 more Tbsp yogurt.
  • Too sweet or too sharp? Balance with a little honey or a little lemon.

Swap

No dairy: unsweetened plain soy or coconut yogurt. Less sugar: use 1 Tbsp honey.

Sauce

S12

Creamy Tomato-Garlic Sauce

Makes 5 portions of sauce (45 g each, about 2-3 Tbsp), stored separatelyActive 5 minTotal 15 min
60kcal
2 gprotein
4 gcarbs
5 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.35
Allergensmilk
Fridge4 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatWarm in a pan over low heat, whisking, or microwave at 50% power 45 sec. Stir.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: C3, C7, C10 (Comfort plates with S12)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1 Tbsp olive oil (13.5 g)
  • 3 cloves garlic, minced (9 g)
  • 3/4 cup tomato sauce (plain, canned) (184 g)
  • 1/2 tsp dried oregano (0.5 g)
  • 3 Tbsp reduced-fat cream cheese, cubed (45 g)
  • 2 Tbsp grated Parmesan (10 g)
  • 1/4 tsp fine salt (1.5 g)

Steps

  1. Warm the oil in a small saucepan over medium-low heat. Add the garlic. Cook 30-60 sec, stirring, until fragrant and just pale gold. Don't let it brown.
  2. Pour in the tomato sauce and add the oregano and salt. Simmer on low 8-10 min, stirring now and then, until it darkens slightly and thickens.
  3. Turn the heat to the lowest setting. Add the cream cheese and whisk until fully melted and the sauce turns a smooth, creamy pink-orange.
  4. Whisk in the Parmesan off the heat. Taste and add salt if it needs it.
  5. Cool 10 min. Spoon into a jar or 5 mini containers (about 2-3 Tbsp / 45 g each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Fire. One burner, 15 min. Low heat after the first 2 minutes.

Fix it

  • Grainy or split? The heat was too high. Whisk in 1 Tbsp warm water off the heat.
  • Too acidic? Stir in 1/4 tsp sugar or honey.

Swap

No dairy: use a plant-based cream cheese and skip the Parmesan (macros change). Lighter: skip the cream cheese and use 3 Tbsp Parmesan.

Chapter 16

48 Signature Plates + the Mix & Match Chart

"It would be nice if prep and recipe and serving sizes were all together so you could pick and chose recipes to batch cook together."

— reader review

A signature plate is one protein, one base, one vegetable and one sauce that we put together on purpose. There are 48, 12 per flavor family. Each one tells you what it adds up to and how to pack it.

Use this chapter two ways:

  • To swap a plate in a plan week. Same parts, different sauce? Find it here and check its numbers.
  • To plan your own week. Pick 5 plates that share 2 proteins, 2 bases and 2 vegetables. Then cook those parts on Sunday.

How to read a plate

  • kcal and protein are for one box built from one standard portion of each part, the portion on its recipe card. Calculated from USDA FoodData Central SR Legacy, including the sauce. Kcal rounded to 10, protein to 1 g. In the plan weeks, some batches are shared by more or fewer boxes, so the week chapters show the numbers for those boxes.
  • Allergens are the 9 major US allergens (FDA) found in any of the 4 parts. Brands differ: read your labels.
  • Fridge is the shortest fridge life of the 4 parts. Count from Sunday (day 0).
  • Freezer tells you if the box can go in the freezer for Friday (the day-5 rule, Chapter 4), and which part comes out softer if it does. Safe, just a different texture.
  • The 3 lines are how you pack and eat it: (1) what goes in the box, (2) what stays apart, (3) how to reheat and finish. The finish (lime, lemon, pepper, scallion) isn't counted in the numbers. It adds almost nothing and wakes up day-4 food.
  • Grams are the standard portion. Weigh the batch and divide it (Chapter 3); the scale wins.

Mexican-style (MEX) · 12 plates

Chipotle, lime, salsa verde, red enchilada. The fridge-friendliest family: most of these sauces freeze, and the yogurt one is never heated.

M1

Chipotle Lemon Chicken Fajita Bowl

P1 Lemon-Garlic Sheet-Pan Chicken Thighs + B1 Hands-Off Oven Rice + V2 Sheet-Pan Fajita Peppers & Onions + S1 Chipotle-Lime Yogurt
640 kcal46 g proteinAllergens: milkFridge: 4 daysFreezer: yes, except S1 (texture)
  1. Box: 160 g rice, 130 g fajita peppers & onions, 136 g lemon-garlic chicken on top.
  2. Apart: 40 g Chipotle-Lime Yogurt.
  3. Microwave covered, stir or turn halfway, until steaming hot: 165 °F / 74 °C. Then sauce cold on top, a squeeze of lime.

Three plates that cross families

Three signature plates break the chart's rule below on purpose: D-8 (sweet potato with a Mediterranean sauce), C5 and C8 (fajita peppers with BBQ sauce). We kept them because the pairing works on the plate and because they let Weeks 1 and 2 reuse the same trays. They're marked here so the chart stays honest.

The Mix & Match chart

You don't need a list of every combination. You need one rule and four small tables.

The rule: pick a sauce. Then any protein, base and vegetable with a ✓ in that sauce's family column goes with it.

That rule gives 1,815 combinations from the 34 parts (10 proteins × 6 bases × 6 vegetables × 12 sauces = 4,320 possible boxes; 1,815 pass the rule). Only the 48 above have names and packing notes. The rest are pairings that follow the same flavor logic, not plates we've built one by one.

Four checks before you commit to a combo

  1. Fridge days. The box keeps as long as its shortest part. Salmon (P8), zucchini (V3) and the cucumber-tomato salad (V6) are 3 days; most parts are 4.
  2. Friday. If the combo is your Friday box, every hot part must freeze. Raw vegetables (V5, V6) and yogurt sauces (S1, S7, S11) don't.
  3. Allergens. Add up the allergens of the four parts. Their recipe cards list them, and so does Chapter 21.
  4. The oven. Two oven proteins at very different temperatures (P7 at 300 °F, everything else at 400-450 °F) don't share a session easily. Week 4 shows the workaround.

Add up any combo yourself

Every recipe card gives kcal and protein per portion. Add the four. Example, a combo that isn't one of the 48:

Part kcal Protein
P3 Never-Dry Baked Chicken Breast 269 45.2 g
B2 Fluffy Quinoa 212 7.2 g
V4 Garlic Green Beans 96 2.7 g
S9 Chimichurri 139 0.5 g
Box 716 → about 720 kcal 55.6 → about 56 g

MED column: P3 ✓, B2 ✓, V4 ✓, S9 is MED. It passes the rule. Allergens: none of the 9 in any part. Fridge: 4 days.

Per-portion numbers from USDA FoodData Central SR Legacy, as printed on each recipe card. The printable "Build Your Plate" sheet does this sum with boxes to fill.

Chapter 17

Breakfasts

"The basic idea holds but it's only for one meal a day..."

— reader review

Lunch and dinner are covered by the boxes. These 8 cover breakfast, cooked in the same Sunday session. Each recipe makes 5, one for each weekday.

How to use this block
  • Each plan week has one breakfast. To swap, pick one that uses the same oven temperature or no oven at all.
  • Egg bites (D1, D2) and baked oats (D6) bake at 350 °F: put them in last, while you pack the boxes.
  • Overnight oats (D3, D4) take 10 minutes and no heat, but need 6 hours in the fridge.
  • Burritos and pancakes (D5, D7) freeze for 3 months. Make a double batch and you have next week's breakfast too.
