Breakfast

D1

Spinach-Feta Egg Bites

Makes 5 portions (3 egg bites per portion, about 150 g)Active 15 minTotal 40 minOven 350 °F / 175 °C
220kcal
18 gprotein
4 gcarbs
15 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.05
Allergensegg, milk
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 2 bites 30-45 sec, 3 bites 45-60 sec, until hot in the middle (165 °F / 74 °C). From frozen: 90 sec at 50% power, then 30 sec at full.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: Breakfast slot. Plan week 1 (Starter). Pairs with a piece of fruit or a slice of toast.

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 10 large eggs (500 g)
  • 1/2 cup 2% cottage cheese (113 g)
  • 4 packed cups fresh baby spinach (120 g)
  • 1/2 cup crumbled feta (75 g)
  • 1 tsp olive oil (for the spinach) (4.5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp garlic powder (1.5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp fine salt (1.5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp black pepper (0.5 g)
  • 1 tsp canola oil or cooking spray (for the tin) (4.5 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 350 °F / 175 °C. Grease 15 muffin cups well (one 12-cup tin plus 3 cups of a second tin) with oil or spray. Silicone cups work too.
  2. Warm the olive oil in a large pan over medium heat. Add the spinach and stir 1-2 min until fully wilted and bright green. Tip into a sieve and press out the water with a spoon. Chop fine.
  3. Blend or whisk the eggs and cottage cheese with the garlic powder, salt and pepper until nearly smooth, with only tiny curds.
  4. Divide the spinach and feta evenly between the cups, then pour in the egg until each is about 3/4 full.
  5. Bake 20-24 min until the tops are puffed and set, the centers don't jiggle when you nudge the tin, and a knife in the middle comes out clean (160 °F / 71 °C).
  6. Cool in the tin 5 min, then run a knife around each and lift out. Cool completely before packing 3 per container.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, 22 min at 350 °F. Slot it in beside the main tray (350 °F on the lower rack) or right after the proteins come out.

Fix it

  • Rubbery or weeping water? It baked too long or the spinach wasn't squeezed dry. Pull at 20 min and squeeze the spinach harder.
  • Stuck to the tin? Grease was too thin. Run a thin knife around each and use silicone cups next time.

Swap

Dairy-free: use 1/2 cup extra spinach and 3 Tbsp nutritional yeast in place of the cottage cheese and feta (macros change). Cheaper: swap the feta for 1/2 cup shredded cheddar.

Breakfast

D2

Turkey & Pepper Egg Bites

Makes 5 portions (3 egg bites per portion, about 170 g)Active 20 minTotal 45 minOven 350 °F / 175 °C
280kcal
24 gprotein
3 gcarbs
19 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.20
Allergensegg, milk
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatMicrowave 2 bites 30-45 sec, 3 bites 45-60 sec, until hot in the middle (165 °F / 74 °C). From frozen: 90 sec at 50% power, then 30 sec at full.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: Breakfast slot. Plan week 4 (Mix-it-yourself). Good with salsa S2 on the side.

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 10 large eggs (500 g)
  • 8 oz ground turkey (93% lean) (227 g)
  • 1 medium red bell pepper, finely diced (120 g)
  • 1/4 cup yellow onion, finely diced (40 g)
  • 1/2 cup shredded sharp cheddar (55 g)
  • 1 tsp olive oil (4.5 g)
  • 1/2 tsp smoked paprika (1.2 g)
  • 3/4 tsp fine salt (4.5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp black pepper (0.5 g)
  • 1 tsp canola oil or cooking spray (for the tin) (4.5 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 350 °F / 175 °C. Grease 15 muffin cups well.
  2. Warm the olive oil in a large pan over medium-high heat. Add the onion and pepper. Cook 3 min until the pepper just softens.
  3. Add the turkey, paprika and half the salt. Break it into small crumbs with a spoon. Cook 5-6 min until no pink remains and a thermometer reads 165 °F / 74 °C. Drain off any liquid. Cool 5 min.
  4. Whisk the eggs with the rest of the salt and the pepper until fully mixed.
  5. Divide the turkey mix and cheddar between the cups. Pour in egg until each is about 3/4 full.
  6. Bake 20-24 min until puffed, set in the middle, and a knife comes out clean (160 °F / 71 °C). Cool 5 min in the tin, lift out, and cool fully before packing 3 per container.

Sunday-sheet slot

Fire first (10 min for the turkey), then oven 22 min at 350 °F beside the main tray.

Fix it

  • Watery bottoms? The turkey liquid wasn't drained. Drain it and blot with a paper towel.
  • Dry? It baked past 24 min. Pull when the centers are just set.

Swap

Dairy-free: leave out the cheddar and add 1/2 tsp extra salt and 1 Tbsp nutritional yeast. Cheaper: use ground chicken or leftover cooked crumble from P4.

Breakfast

D3

Greek Yogurt Overnight Oats

Makes 5 portions (1 jar, about 330 g)Active 10 minTotal 10 min + 6 h chill
340kcal
24 gprotein
50 gcarbs
6 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.95
Allergensmilk
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatEat cold, straight from the jar. Or microwave 60-90 sec and stir.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: Breakfast slot. Plan week 2 (Budget). Takes no hands on the morning.

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 1/2 cups old-fashioned rolled oats (200 g)
  • 2 1/2 cups plain nonfat Greek yogurt (600 g)
  • 2 1/2 cups 1% milk (610 g)
  • 2 Tbsp + 2 tsp chia seeds (20 g)
  • 5 tsp honey (35 g)
  • 1 tsp vanilla extract (4 g)
  • 1 tsp ground cinnamon (2.6 g)
  • a pinch fine salt (0.5 g)
  • 1 1/4 cups blueberries (fresh or frozen) (185 g)

Steps

  1. Line up 5 jars or containers of at least 12 oz / 350 ml each.
  2. In a large bowl, stir together the oats, chia, cinnamon and salt.
  3. Add the yogurt, milk, honey and vanilla. Stir until there are no dry pockets of oats and no yogurt lumps.
  4. Divide evenly between the jars (about 1 cup / 285 g each).
  5. Top each jar with 1/4 cup berries. Close the lids and refrigerate at least 6 hours.
  6. In the morning, stir the jar. It's ready when the oats are soft and the mix looks thick and spoonable like pudding.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 10 minutes, any time on Sunday. Needs 6 hours in the fridge, so make it before dinner.

Fix it

  • Too thick? Stir in 2 Tbsp milk before eating.
  • Too runny? The oats were quick oats or the jar sat under 6 hours. Add 1 tsp chia and wait 30 min.

Swap

Dairy-free: use unsweetened soy milk and a plain soy or coconut yogurt (macros change). Cheaper: use frozen berries or 1 mashed banana instead.

Breakfast

D4

Peanut Butter-Banana Overnight Oats

Makes 5 portions (1 jar, about 290 g)Active 10 minTotal 10 min + 6 h chill
400kcal
22 gprotein
51 gcarbs
14 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.10
Allergensmilk, peanuts
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (texture)
ReheatEat cold. Or microwave 60-90 sec, stir, and add a splash of milk.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: Breakfast slot. Banana is mashed in, so it doesn't brown. Add a few banana slices on top the morning you eat it if you like.

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 1/2 cups old-fashioned rolled oats (200 g)
  • 2 1/2 cups 1% milk (610 g)
  • 1 1/4 cups plain nonfat Greek yogurt (300 g)
  • 5 Tbsp smooth peanut butter (no sugar added) (80 g)
  • 2 medium ripe bananas, mashed (236 g)
  • 2 Tbsp + 2 tsp chia seeds (20 g)
  • 1 tsp vanilla extract (4 g)
  • 1 tsp ground cinnamon (2.6 g)
  • a pinch fine salt (0.5 g)

Steps

  1. Line up 5 jars of at least 12 oz / 350 ml each.
  2. In a large bowl, mash the bananas with a fork until almost smooth. Stir in the peanut butter until it's mostly blended.
  3. Whisk in the milk, then the yogurt, vanilla, cinnamon and salt until the mix is an even light tan.
  4. Stir in the oats and chia.
  5. Divide evenly between the jars (about 1 1/4 cups / 290 g each). Close and refrigerate at least 6 hours.
  6. In the morning, stir. It's ready when the oats are soft and no dry oats show at the bottom.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 10 minutes, any time. Needs 6 hours in the fridge, so make it before dinner.

Fix it

  • Peanut butter lumps? Warm the spoon in hot water or microwave the peanut butter 10 sec before mixing.
  • Too thick? Stir in 2 Tbsp milk. Too sweet? Use one banana less.

Swap

Peanut allergy: use sunflower seed butter (macros change). Dairy-free: soy milk and plain soy yogurt (macros change).

Breakfast

D5

Freezer Breakfast Burritos

Makes 5 portions (1 burrito, about 255 g)Active 25 minTotal 35 min
520kcal
28 gprotein
43 gcarbs
26 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.50
Allergensegg, milk, wheat
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatFrom frozen: unwrap, wrap in a damp paper towel and microwave 2 min, flip, then 45-60 sec more until hot in the middle (165 °F / 74 °C). Or thaw overnight in the fridge and microwave 60-90 sec.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: Breakfast slot. Plan week 3 (Freezer-ahead). Serve with salsa S2 or S1 on the side (not counted).

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 10 large eggs (500 g)
  • 5 10-inch flour tortillas (325 g)
  • 1 cup canned low-sodium black beans, drained and rinsed (215 g)
  • 1 1/4 cups shredded sharp cheddar (140 g)
  • 1 medium red bell pepper, finely diced (120 g)
  • 1/3 cup yellow onion, finely diced (50 g)
  • 2 tsp olive oil (9 g)
  • 1/2 tsp ground cumin (1 g)
  • 3/4 tsp fine salt (4.5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp black pepper (0.5 g)

Steps

  1. Warm the oil in a large nonstick pan over medium heat. Cook the onion and pepper 3-4 min until the edges soften. Stir in the cumin, salt and pepper.
  2. Beat the eggs in a bowl. Pour them into the pan and stir gently, folding, until the eggs form large soft curds and no wet shine is left, 3-4 min. Take it off the heat; it keeps cooking.
  3. Cool the egg mix 10 min on a plate. Hot filling steams the tortillas soggy.
  4. Warm the tortillas 10 sec each in the microwave so they fold without cracking. Lay one flat and put 1/5 of the eggs, 3 Tbsp beans and 1/5 of the cheddar in a line below the center.
  5. Fold the sides in, then roll up tight from the bottom. Repeat for all 5.
  6. Wrap each burrito in parchment or foil. For the freezer, put them in a bag, press out the air and flatten. Label with the date.

Sunday-sheet slot

Fire. One pan, 15 min of active time. Do it while the oven is full; no oven needed.

Fix it

  • Soggy burrito? The filling was warm when you rolled. Cool it fully first and drain the beans well.
  • Eggs dry after reheating? Cook them softer (still set, a little glossy) and reheat covered with a damp towel.

Swap

Dairy-free: omit the cheddar and add 1/2 cup more beans. Gluten-free: use large corn tortillas or serve as a bowl (macros change). More protein: add 4 oz / 115 g cooked turkey crumble (P4) to the mix.

Breakfast

D6

Cottage Cheese Baked Oats

Makes 5 portions (1 large square, about 255 g)Active 10 minTotal 60 minOven 350 °F / 175 °C
370kcal
22 gprotein
50 gcarbs
10 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.90
Allergensegg, milk
Fridge4 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatMicrowave a square 60-90 sec until hot in the middle. Or 350 °F / 175 °C oven 10 min, covered. Good cold too.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: Breakfast slot. Tastes like dessert. Cut into 5 on Sunday and it reheats well all week.

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2 cups old-fashioned rolled oats (170 g)
  • 2 cups 2% cottage cheese (450 g)
  • 4 large eggs (200 g)
  • 2 medium ripe bananas (236 g)
  • 1/2 cup 1% milk (122 g)
  • 2 Tbsp pure maple syrup (40 g)
  • 1 1/2 tsp baking powder (7 g)
  • 2 tsp vanilla extract (9 g)
  • 2 tsp ground cinnamon (5 g)
  • 1/4 tsp fine salt (1.5 g)
  • 1 cup blueberries (fresh or frozen) (150 g)
  • 1 tsp cooking spray or butter (for the dish) (4.5 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 350 °F / 175 °C. Grease a 9x13 inch / 23x33 cm baking dish.
  2. Blend the cottage cheese, eggs, bananas, milk, maple syrup, vanilla, baking powder, cinnamon and salt in a blender until smooth and pourable, about 30 sec.
  3. Stir in the oats in a large bowl until all the oats are wet.
  4. Pour into the dish and spread level. Scatter the blueberries over the top, pressing in lightly.
  5. Bake 40-45 min until the edges are golden, the middle doesn't jiggle, and a knife in the center comes out clean (160 °F / 71 °C).
  6. Cool 15 min. Cut into 5 squares (or more, thinner). Cool completely before packing.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, 45 min at 350 °F. Put it on the lower rack beside the main tray or in first thing.

Fix it

  • Wet in the middle? Bake 5-8 min more. Cover the top with foil if it browns too fast.
  • Dry or rubbery edges? Your oven runs hot or the dish was smaller than 9x13. Check at 35 min next time.

Swap

Cheaper: use frozen berries. Dairy-free: not a good fit (cottage cheese is the structure); try D3 with plant yogurt. Less sweet: use 1 Tbsp maple syrup.

D7

Freezer Protein Pancakes

Makes 5 portions (3 pancakes, about 150 g)Active 30 minTotal 40 min
230kcal
16 gprotein
22 gcarbs
9 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.95
Allergensegg, milk
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatToaster or toaster oven (375 °F / 190 °C) 4-5 min until hot and crisp at the edges. Or microwave 3 pancakes 45-60 sec. From frozen: add 1 min.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: Breakfast slot. Syrup, peanut butter or fruit are extra and not counted.

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1 cup old-fashioned rolled oats (100 g)
  • 1 1/2 cups 2% cottage cheese (340 g)
  • 4 large eggs (200 g)
  • 1 small ripe banana (100 g)
  • 1 1/2 tsp baking powder (7 g)
  • 1 tsp vanilla extract (4.5 g)
  • 1 tsp ground cinnamon (2.6 g)
  • 1/4 tsp fine salt (1.5 g)
  • 2 tsp canola oil (for the pan) (9 g)

Steps

  1. Blend the oats, cottage cheese, eggs, banana, baking powder, vanilla, cinnamon and salt in a blender 30-40 sec until smooth. Rest the batter 5 min so the oats soften.
  2. Heat a nonstick pan or griddle over medium heat. Brush with a little oil.
  3. Pour 3 Tbsp / 45 g of batter per pancake, about 4 inches wide. Cook 2-3 min until bubbles form and pop on top and the edges look dry.
  4. Flip. Cook 1-2 min until golden underneath and the center springs back (160 °F / 71 °C). Re-oil the pan every batch. Make 15 pancakes.
  5. Cool the pancakes in a single layer on a rack, 10 min. Stacked warm pancakes steam and stick.
  6. Pack 3 per portion. For the freezer, freeze flat on a tray 1 hour, then bag them with parchment between layers.

Sunday-sheet slot

Fire. One pan, about 25 min of active time. Do it while the oven bakes everything else.

Fix it

  • Pancakes tear when flipped? Wait for bubbles to pop across the surface and the edges to look dry, and use a thin spatula.
  • Raw center? The heat is too high. Drop to medium-low and make them smaller.

Swap

Gluten-free: use certified gluten-free oats. More protein: replace 2 Tbsp of the oats with 2 Tbsp unflavored or vanilla protein powder (macros change). No banana: use 1/4 cup unsweetened applesauce.

Breakfast

D8

Turkey Sausage & Sweet Potato Hash

Makes 5 portions (1 heaping cup, about 230 g)Active 20 minTotal 50 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
310kcal
21 gprotein
30 gcarbs
13 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.70
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatSkillet over medium heat 3-4 min, or microwave 90 sec, stirring once, until steaming and 165 °F / 74 °C.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: Breakfast slot. Top with a fried or poached egg the morning you eat it (not counted).

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 1 lb ground turkey (93% lean) (454 g)
  • 2 medium sweet potatoes, peeled, 1/2-inch cubes (600 g)
  • 1 large yellow onion, diced (150 g)
  • 1 large red bell pepper, diced (150 g)
  • 2 Tbsp olive oil (27 g)
  • 2 tsp smoked paprika (4.6 g)
  • 1 tsp dried thyme (1 g)
  • 1 tsp garlic powder (3 g)
  • 1 1/2 tsp fine salt (9 g)
  • 1/2 tsp black pepper (1 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C with two rimmed sheet pans inside.
  2. Toss the sweet potato, onion and pepper with the oil, paprika, thyme, garlic powder, salt and pepper in a big bowl.
  3. Spread over the two hot pans in one layer with space between. Roast 15 min until the potato edges just start to brown.
  4. Pull the pans out. Crumble the raw turkey in small clumps over the vegetables, then stir roughly.
  5. Roast 15-18 min more, stirring once at the halfway point, until the turkey has no pink, the potato is soft in the middle and crisp at the edges, and a thermometer in the turkey reads 165 °F / 74 °C.
  6. Cool 15 min on the pans. Mix well, divide into 5 containers (about 1 heaping cup each).

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven, 35 min at 425 °F. Use the oven slot when the proteins and veg are not full; fits with P1 or V1 at the same temperature.

Fix it

  • Soft, not crisp? The pans were crowded. Use two pans and don't stack the vegetables.
  • Dry turkey? It stayed in too long. Break the clumps smaller next time so it cooks faster.

Swap

Cheaper: use regular ground pork or beef (reheat to 160 °F / 71 °C; macros change). More veg: add 2 cups chopped kale in the last 5 min.

Chapter 18

The Freezer Block · 10 One-Pot Meals

"Keep em in the freezer and you're good for weeks."

— reader review

For the weeks when Sunday doesn't happen. Each recipe is one pot or one dish, makes 6 portions, and is built to freeze. Cook one on any free evening, portion it, freeze it, and take out one box at a time.

How to use this block
  • This is Path C from Chapter 0. You can live on this block alone for a while.
  • Freeze on the day you cook. Not on day 3 (freezing doesn't reset the clock).
  • Shallow containers, 2 inches (5 cm) deep or less, so they cool fast and thaw evenly.
  • Thaw in the fridge overnight, never on the counter. Reheat until 165 °F / 74 °C in the center.
  • Label every box with the name and date. Quality is best within the months in the table.
ID Recipe Portion kcal Protein Fridge Freezer Allergens

FM2 Chicken Tikka-Style Curry 525 g 550 46 g 3 days 3 mo milk
FM3 Beef & Lentil Ragù 490 g 560 37 g 4 days 3 mo wheat, milk

Freezer meal

FM1

Turkey Three-Bean Chili

Makes 6 portions (~540 g each)Active 25 minTotal 60 min
480kcal
42 gprotein
44 gcarbs
18 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $3.20
Allergensnone
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Reheat covered in the microwave 3-4 min, stirring halfway, or in a pot over medium 6-8 min, until steaming and 165 °F / 74 °C in the center.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: B1 Hands-Off Oven Rice, S1 Chipotle-Lime Yogurt, V6 Chopped Cucumber-Tomato Salad

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • lean ground turkey (93/7): 2 lb (907 g)
  • olive oil: 1 tbsp (14 g)
  • yellow onion, diced: 1 large (200 g)
  • red bell pepper, diced: 1 large (150 g)
  • garlic, minced: 4 cloves (12 g)
  • chili powder: 2 tbsp (15 g)
  • ground cumin: 2 tsp (5 g)
  • smoked paprika: 1 tsp (3 g)
  • crushed tomatoes: 1 can (28 oz) (794 g)
  • tomato paste: 2 tbsp (32 g)
  • kidney beans, canned, drained and rinsed: 1 can (15 oz) (240 g)
  • black beans, canned, drained and rinsed: 1 can (15 oz) (240 g)
  • pinto beans, canned, drained and rinsed: 1 can (15 oz) (240 g)
  • frozen corn kernels: 1 cup (150 g)
  • low-sodium chicken broth: 2 cups (480 g)
  • kosher salt: 1 tsp (5 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oil in a large pot (5-6 qt / 5-6 L) over medium-high heat. Add the onion and pepper and cook 5 minutes, stirring, until the onion is soft and see-through at the edges.
  2. Add the garlic and cook 30 seconds, just until you can smell it. Push the vegetables to the sides, add the turkey, and break it up with a spoon. Cook 6-8 minutes until no pink is left and it looks like small crumbles.
  3. Stir in the chili powder, cumin, paprika and tomato paste. Cook 1 minute until the paste darkens a shade and the spices smell toasty.
  4. Add the crushed tomatoes, all three beans, corn, broth and salt. Bring to a boil, then drop to low and simmer, partly covered, 30 minutes, stirring every 10 minutes. It is done when a spoon dragged across the bottom leaves a clear trail for a second and the chili coats the spoon.
  5. Taste and add salt in pinches if needed. Check that the chili is at least 165 °F / 74 °C in the middle.
  6. Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  7. Portion and freeze: divide into 6 labeled containers (date on the lid), leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace, and freeze flat. Chili freezes well in zip bags laid flat: they stack and thaw in half the time.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop. Start at minute 0 on a back burner; it simmers on its own from minute 20 to minute 50, so the oven and the other burners stay free for P and B recipes.

Fix it

  • Too thin: simmer uncovered 10 more minutes, or mash a half cup of the beans against the pot wall and stir them in.
  • Tastes flat: add 1 tsp vinegar or lime juice off the heat and a pinch more salt. Acid wakes up tomato-based dishes.

Swap

Swap the turkey for 2 lb of 90% lean ground beef (brown it until no pink remains and the internal temp reads 160 °F / 71 °C). For a budget version, replace one can of beans with 1 cup of cooked lentils.

Freezer meal

FM2

Chicken Tikka-Style Curry

Makes 6 portions (~525 g each)Active 25 minTotal 55 min
550kcal
46 gprotein
49 gcarbs
20 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.85
Allergensmilk (coconut milk too)
Fridge3 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Microwave covered 3-4 min, stirring halfway, adding 1 tbsp water to the rice; or reheat the curry in a pan over medium 6-8 min. It must reach 165 °F / 74 °C in the center.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: B1 Hands-Off Oven Rice (the rice is already packed in), V5 Sesame Cabbage-Carrot Slaw, S7 Tzatziki

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • boneless skinless chicken thighs, cut in 1.5 in chunks: 2.2 lb (1000 g)
  • plain nonfat Greek yogurt: 1 cup (245 g)
  • curry powder: 1 tbsp (7 g)
  • kosher salt: 1.5 tsp (8 g)
  • olive oil: 1 tbsp (14 g)
  • yellow onion, diced: 1 large (250 g)
  • garlic, minced: 4 cloves (12 g)
  • fresh ginger, grated: 1 tbsp (10 g)
  • smoked paprika: 2 tsp (5 g)
  • ground turmeric: 1 tsp (3 g)
  • tomato paste: 3 tbsp (48 g)
  • crushed tomatoes: 1 can (14.5 oz) (411 g)
  • canned coconut milk: 1 cup (half a can) (240 g)
  • chickpeas, canned, drained and rinsed: 1 can (15 oz) (240 g)
  • fresh baby spinach: 5 oz (140 g)
  • long-grain white rice, cooked (to pack with each portion): about 1/2 cup / 85 g per portion (510 g)

Nutrition note: includes 85 g of cooked rice per portion

Steps

  1. Mix the chicken with the yogurt, curry powder and 1 tsp of the salt in a bowl. Let it sit 15 minutes on the counter while you chop (no longer than 30 minutes; for longer, marinate in the fridge).
  2. Heat the oil in a large deep skillet or Dutch oven over medium-high heat. Add the onion and cook 5 minutes until soft with golden edges. Add garlic and ginger and cook 30 seconds.
  3. Stir in paprika, turmeric and tomato paste. Cook 1-2 minutes until the paste turns a deeper brick red.
  4. Add the chicken with all its marinade, the crushed tomatoes and the coconut milk. Stir, bring to a gentle bubble, then lower to medium-low and simmer, partly covered, 15 minutes. The chicken is done when the biggest piece is white all the way through and reads 165 °F / 74 °C.
  5. Stir in the chickpeas and spinach and the rest of the salt. Cook 2-3 minutes until the spinach has wilted into the sauce and the sauce coats the back of a spoon. Do not boil hard once the yogurt is in, or it can split.
  6. Cook the rice (or use the rice from B1). Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  7. Portion and freeze: pack each of 6 containers with a scoop of rice (about 1/2 cup / 85 g) and a scoop of curry, side by side. Date the lids and freeze flat.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop. Starts at minute 10 (after the oven things are in) and needs about 40 minutes with the lid half on, so it can share the back burner with a rice pot.

Fix it

  • Sauce split or grainy: you boiled it after the yogurt went in. Take it off the heat, whisk in 2 tbsp warm water, and keep it at a bare simmer from now on.
  • Chicken tough: the chunks were too small or cooked too hot. Cut them at least 1.5 in / 4 cm and keep the simmer gentle.

Swap

Swap thighs for boneless skinless chicken breast (same weight); cook it just until it hits 165 °F / 74 °C. Dairy-free: use coconut yogurt for the marinade and skip nothing else. Nut-free note: coconut is not a tree nut in most guidance, but check with your doctor if you have a tree-nut allergy.

Freezer meal

FM3

Beef & Lentil Ragù

Makes 6 portions (~490 g each)Active 25 minTotal 65 min
560kcal
37 gprotein
72 gcarbs
15 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $3.05
Allergenswheat, milk
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Reheat in a pot over medium 6-8 min (or microwave covered 3-4 min), stirring, until 165 °F / 74 °C in the center. Meanwhile boil 1.75 oz / 50 g dry pasta per portion for the time on the package minus 1 minute, and toss it with the hot ragù. Nutrition below includes this pasta and the Parmesan.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: B5 Reheat-Ready Pasta (use instead of fresh pasta if you prefer), V1 Charred Roasted Broccoli, V3 Roasted Zucchini & Red Onion

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • lean ground beef (90/10): 1.25 lb (567 g)
  • olive oil: 1 tbsp (14 g)
  • yellow onion, finely diced: 1 large (200 g)
  • carrots, finely diced: 2 medium (130 g)
  • celery, finely diced: 2 stalks (80 g)
  • garlic, minced: 4 cloves (12 g)
  • tomato paste: 3 tbsp (48 g)
  • dried oregano: 2 tsp (3 g)
  • brown or green lentils, dry, rinsed: 3/4 cup (150 g)
  • crushed tomatoes: 1 can (28 oz) (794 g)
  • low-sodium beef broth: 2 cups (480 g)
  • kosher salt: 1 tsp (5 g)
  • black pepper: 1/2 tsp (1 g)
  • dry spaghetti or penne (cooked fresh on serving day, about 1.75 oz / 50 g dry per portion): about 10.5 oz total (300 g)
  • grated Parmesan (1 tbsp per portion): 6 tbsp (30 g)

Nutrition note: includes the pasta (50 g dry per portion) and 1 tbsp Parmesan, added on serving day

Steps

  1. Heat the oil in a large pot over medium-high heat. Add the beef and break it into small crumbles. Cook 7-8 minutes until all the pink is gone and the bits are lightly browned. Spoon off most of the fat.
  2. Add onion, carrot and celery. Cook 6 minutes, stirring, until the onion is soft and see-through. Add garlic and oregano, 30 seconds.
  3. Stir in the tomato paste and cook 1 minute until it darkens a shade.
  4. Add the lentils, crushed tomatoes, broth, salt and pepper. Bring to a boil, lower to a steady simmer, cover with the lid slightly open, and cook 35-40 minutes, stirring every 10 minutes. It is done when a lentil squashes easily between two fingers and the sauce is thick enough to hold a spoon trail.
  5. If it looks dry before the lentils are soft, add 1/2 cup water and keep going. Check that it reads 160 °F / 71 °C or more in the center.
  6. Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  7. Portion and freeze: divide into 6 labeled containers (date on the lid), leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace, and freeze flat. Freeze the ragù only. Do not freeze it mixed with pasta; pasta turns mushy.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop. Start at minute 5; it simmers hands-off from minute 25 to minute 65, which suits the long, quiet part of a Sunday.

Fix it

  • Lentils still hard after 40 minutes: your lentils are old or your tomatoes are very acidic. Add 1/2 cup water, cover, and cook 10 more minutes before tasting again.
  • Greasy surface: skim it off with a spoon before portioning, or chill the ragù and lift off the white fat that sets on top.

Swap

Swap the beef for lean ground turkey (cook to 165 °F / 74 °C). Gluten-free: serve over gluten-free pasta or baked sweet potato. Budget: use 1 lb of beef and 1 cup (200 g) lentils.

Freezer meal

FM4

Chicken Enchilada Bake

Makes 6 portions (~390 g each)Active 30 minTotal 70 minOven 375 °F / 190 °C
550kcal
50 gprotein
45 gcarbs
19 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $3.20
Allergensmilk
Fridge3 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Microwave 2-3 min on a plate, covered, until steaming; or bake at 350 °F / 175 °C for 15-20 min covered with foil. Center must reach 165 °F / 74 °C.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: V2 Sheet-Pan Fajita Peppers & Onions, B1 Hands-Off Oven Rice, S1 Chipotle-Lime Yogurt

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • boneless skinless chicken breasts: 2 lb (907 g)
  • red enchilada sauce, mild: 3 cups (24 oz) (680 g)
  • olive oil: 1 tbsp (14 g)
  • yellow onion, diced: 1 medium (150 g)
  • ground cumin: 2 tsp (5 g)
  • chili powder: 1 tbsp (8 g)
  • black beans, canned, drained and rinsed: 1 can (15 oz) (240 g)
  • frozen corn kernels: 1 cup (150 g)
  • corn tortillas (6 in): 12 (312 g)
  • shredded Mexican-blend cheese: 1.5 cups (6 oz) (170 g)
  • plain nonfat Greek yogurt (stirred into the filling): 1/2 cup (125 g)
  • kosher salt: 1/2 tsp (3 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 375 °F / 190 °C. Lightly oil a 9x13 in / 23x33 cm baking dish.
  2. In a large skillet, heat the oil over medium and cook the onion 4 minutes until soft. Add cumin and chili powder for 30 seconds. Pour in 2 cups of the enchilada sauce, add the whole chicken breasts, cover, and simmer on low 18-25 minutes, flipping once, until the thickest part reads 165 °F / 74 °C and the juices run clear.
  3. Move the chicken to a board and shred it with two forks. Return it to the skillet with the beans, corn and salt, and stir. Take the pan off the heat and stir in the yogurt (off the heat so it does not curdle).
  4. Spread 1/2 cup sauce on the bottom of the dish. Layer 4 tortillas (overlap them), then 1/3 of the filling, then 1/4 of the cheese. Repeat twice. Finish with the last 4 tortillas, the last of the sauce, and the rest of the cheese.
  5. Cover with foil and bake 20 minutes. Uncover and bake 10-15 minutes more until the cheese is melted with a few browned spots and the sauce bubbles at the edges. The center must read 165 °F / 74 °C.
  6. Let it rest 10 minutes so it cuts cleanly into 6 squares. Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  7. Portion and freeze: divide into 6 labeled containers (date on the lid), leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace, and freeze flat. Wrap each square individually in parchment, then put in a freezer bag or container.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven for 30-35 min. Slot it right after the sheet-pan proteins come out (425 °F to 375 °F: let the oven drop 10 minutes). The chicken simmers on the stove before that.

Fix it

  • Soggy bottom: the filling was too wet. Simmer the shredded chicken 2 minutes uncovered before layering, and rest the bake 10 minutes before cutting.
  • Tortillas tear while layering: warm them 20 seconds in the microwave wrapped in a damp towel.

Swap

Swap the chicken for 2 lb of lean ground turkey, browned to 165 °F / 74 °C, or for 3 cups of extra beans plus 1 package of crumbled firm tofu (vegetarian). Use dairy-free cheese for a dairy-free version.

Freezer meal

FM5

Turkey Sausage & Lentil Soup

Makes 6 portions (~555 g each)Active 20 minTotal 65 min
560kcal
28 gprotein
50 gcarbs
30 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.70
Allergenswheat
Fridge4 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Reheat in a pot over medium 6-8 min, adding 1/4 cup water or broth per portion because the barley keeps drinking liquid, until steaming and 165 °F / 74 °C in the center. Microwave covered 3-4 min, stirring halfway.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: B6 Wraps & Tortillas (for dipping, toasted), V6 Chopped Cucumber-Tomato Salad, S9 Chimichurri (a spoonful on top)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • fully cooked smoked turkey sausage, sliced into coins: 1 lb (454 g)
  • olive oil: 1.5 tbsp (21 g)
  • yellow onion, diced: 1 large (200 g)
  • carrots, diced: 3 medium (200 g)
  • celery, diced: 3 stalks (120 g)
  • garlic, minced: 4 cloves (12 g)
  • dried thyme: 1 tsp (2 g)
  • brown or green lentils, dry, rinsed: 1.25 cups (250 g)
  • diced tomatoes with their juice: 1 can (14.5 oz) (411 g)
  • low-sodium chicken broth: 7 cups (1680 g)
  • kale, stems removed, chopped: 3 cups packed (90 g)
  • pearled barley: 1/4 cup (50 g)
  • kosher salt: 1 tsp (5 g)

Steps

  1. Heat 2 tsp of the oil in a large pot over medium-high heat. Add the sausage coins and cook 4-5 minutes until browned on both sides. Take them out and set aside.
  2. Add the rest of the oil, onion, carrot and celery. Cook 6 minutes until the onion is soft and see-through. Add garlic and thyme for 30 seconds.
  3. Add the lentils, barley, tomatoes with juice, broth and salt. Bring to a boil, lower to a steady simmer, and partly cover. Cook 30 minutes.
  4. Add the sausage back and the kale. Simmer 10 more minutes. The soup is done when the lentils and barley are tender with no hard center and the kale is dark green and soft.
  5. Taste and adjust the salt. The soup will thicken as it cools; that is normal. The soup must read 165 °F / 74 °C or more before you portion.
  6. Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  7. Portion and freeze: divide into 6 labeled containers (date on the lid), leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace, and freeze flat. Leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace; soup expands. Freeze in zip bags laid flat if short on space.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop. Start at minute 10; simmers on its own from minute 25. Pairs with a long oven item like P2 or P7.

Fix it

  • Soup turned into stew overnight: the barley and lentils absorbed the broth. Add broth or water when reheating until it looks like soup again.
  • Lentils split into mush: you cooked too hard. Keep a lazy simmer with small bubbles, not a rolling boil.

Swap

Swap the sausage for 1.5 cups of diced cooked chicken breast added in the last 5 minutes, or for a second can of chickpeas (vegetarian, check the broth). Gluten-free: leave out the barley and add 1/2 cup more lentils.

Freezer meal

FM6

Chicken Tortilla Soup

Makes 6 portions (~655 g each)Active 20 minTotal 50 min
560kcal
58 gprotein
43 gcarbs
19 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $3.60
Allergensmilk
Fridge3 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Reheat in a pot over medium 6-8 min or microwave covered 3-4 min, until 165 °F / 74 °C in the center. Add toppings after heating.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: B1 Hands-Off Oven Rice (a scoop in the bowl), S1 Chipotle-Lime Yogurt, V6 Chopped Cucumber-Tomato Salad

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • boneless skinless chicken breasts: 2.2 lb (1000 g)
  • olive oil: 1 tbsp (14 g)
  • yellow onion, diced: 1 large (200 g)
  • jalapeño, seeded and minced: 1 (30 g)
  • garlic, minced: 4 cloves (12 g)
  • chili powder: 1 tbsp (8 g)
  • ground cumin: 2 tsp (5 g)
  • diced tomatoes with green chilies: 1 can (14.5 oz) (411 g)
  • low-sodium chicken broth: 6 cups (1440 g)
  • black beans, canned, drained and rinsed: 1 can (15 oz) (240 g)
  • pinto beans, canned, drained and rinsed: 1 can (15 oz) (240 g)
  • frozen corn kernels: 1 cup (150 g)
  • lime juice: 3 tbsp (45 g)
  • fresh cilantro, chopped: 1/2 cup (15 g)
  • kosher salt: 1 tsp (5 g)
  • corn tortillas, cut in strips (topping, baked fresh on serving day): 6 (156 g)
  • shredded Monterey Jack (1 oz per portion): 6 oz (170 g)

Nutrition note: includes the tortilla strips and 28 g of cheese topping

Steps

  1. Heat the oil in a large pot over medium-high heat. Cook the onion and jalapeño 5 minutes until soft. Add garlic, chili powder and cumin for 30 seconds.
  2. Add the tomatoes with chilies and the broth. Lay the whole chicken breasts in the pot. Bring to a boil, then lower to a gentle simmer, cover, and cook 18-20 minutes until the thickest breast reads 165 °F / 74 °C and is white inside.
  3. Move the chicken to a board and shred with two forks. Return it to the pot with both beans and the corn. Simmer 5 more minutes.
  4. Off the heat, stir in the lime juice, cilantro and salt. Taste: it should taste bright, not flat.
  5. Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  6. Portion and freeze: divide into 6 labeled containers (date on the lid), leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace, and freeze flat. Freeze the soup only. The tortilla strips and cheese are added on serving day.
  7. Serving-day topping (macros include it): toss the strips with a little spray oil, bake at 400 °F / 200 °C for 8-10 minutes until crisp and light golden. Top each bowl with the strips and 1 oz / 28 g cheese.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop. Start at minute 15; 35 minutes of mostly waiting. The tortilla strips go in the oven for 10 minutes on the day you eat it, not on Sunday.

Fix it

  • Chicken stringy and dry: it boiled hard. Keep the simmer lazy and pull the breasts out the minute they hit 165 °F / 74 °C.
  • Soup tastes like plain broth: add salt a pinch at a time and another splash of lime juice at the end, never before the simmer.

Swap

Swap chicken breast for thighs (same weight) for juicier meat; they need about 25 minutes. Dairy-free: skip the cheese and top with sliced avocado on serving day.

Freezer meal

FM7

Teriyaki Chicken Fried Rice

Makes 6 portions (~310 g each)Active 30 minTotal 45 min
500kcal
38 gprotein
54 gcarbs
14 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.80
Allergensegg, soy, wheat, sesame
Fridge3 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Microwave covered with 1 tbsp water 2-3 min, stirring halfway, until steaming and 165 °F / 74 °C in the center; or reheat in a skillet over medium-high 4-5 min.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: V1 Charred Roasted Broccoli, V5 Sesame Cabbage-Carrot Slaw, S6 Gochujang-Honey

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • long-grain white rice, dry (cook ahead and chill): 1.5 cups (300 g)
  • boneless skinless chicken breasts, cut in 3/4 in cubes: 1.5 lb (700 g)
  • canola oil: 2 tbsp (28 g)
  • toasted sesame oil: 1 tbsp (14 g)
  • large eggs, beaten: 4 (200 g)
  • frozen mixed vegetables (peas, carrots, corn, green beans): 1 bag (14 oz) (400 g)
  • scallions, sliced: 4 (50 g)
  • garlic, minced: 3 cloves (9 g)
  • fresh ginger, grated: 1 tbsp (10 g)
  • teriyaki sauce: 6 tbsp (100 g)
  • low-sodium soy sauce: 2 tbsp (36 g)

Steps

  1. Cook the rice ahead: 1.5 cups rice with 3 cups water, covered, 15-18 minutes until all the water is absorbed. Spread it on a sheet pan to cool 20 minutes, then chill it uncovered in the fridge at least 1 hour (cold, dry rice fries better and stays safe). Use it within 24 hours.
  2. Heat 1 tbsp canola oil in your largest skillet or wok over high heat. Add the chicken in a single layer and cook 5-6 minutes, turning, until golden and white inside (165 °F / 74 °C). Move to a bowl.
  3. Add the other 1 tbsp canola oil, pour in the eggs, and scramble 1-2 minutes until fully set with no runny parts. Move them to the chicken bowl.
  4. Add the sesame oil, garlic, ginger and the white parts of the scallions. Cook 30 seconds. Add the frozen vegetables and cook 3 minutes until hot and bright.
  5. Add the cold rice, breaking up clumps. Stir-fry 4 minutes until the rice is hot and a few grains look toasted. Pour in the teriyaki and soy sauce and toss 1 minute.
  6. Return the chicken and eggs. Toss 1 minute until everything is steaming. Stir in the green scallion tops. If your pan is small, cook in 2 batches. Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  7. Portion and freeze: divide into 6 labeled containers (date on the lid), leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace, and freeze flat. Fried rice freezes best in flat, thin layers; press it to about 1 in / 2.5 cm thick.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop, last. Cook the rice the night before, so on Sunday this is a 20 minute active job once the other burners are free.

Fix it

  • Mushy, clumpy rice: the rice was warm or too wet. Chill it fully, uncovered, and use a hot pan.
  • Bland: add 1 tsp soy sauce at the end, not more teriyaki (it is sweet). A squeeze of lime lifts it.

Swap

Swap the rice for quinoa (B2) or cauliflower rice (lower carbs, cut the rice and add 4 cups). Gluten-free: use tamari and a gluten-free teriyaki.

Freezer meal

FM8

Turkey Meatball & Orzo Bake

Makes 6 portions (~380 g each)Active 30 minTotal 65 minOven 400 °F / 205 °C
530kcal
39 gprotein
53 gcarbs
19 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.90
Allergensegg, wheat, milk
Fridge3 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Microwave covered 3-4 min with 1 tbsp water on top, or bake covered at 350 °F / 175 °C for 15-20 min, until 165 °F / 74 °C in the center.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: V3 Roasted Zucchini & Red Onion, V4 Garlic Green Beans, S9 Chimichurri

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • lean ground turkey (93/7): 1.5 lb (680 g)
  • large egg: 1 (50 g)
  • plain dry breadcrumbs: 1/3 cup (40 g)
  • grated Parmesan: 1/4 cup (25 g)
  • yellow onion, grated: 1/2 medium (75 g)
  • garlic, minced: 4 cloves (12 g)
  • dried oregano: 1 tsp (2 g)
  • kosher salt: 1 tsp (5 g)
  • marinara sauce, no sugar added: 1 jar (24 oz) (680 g)
  • low-sodium chicken broth: 2 cups (480 g)
  • dry orzo (or other small pasta): 1.5 cups (270 g)
  • baby spinach, chopped: 4 oz (115 g)
  • shredded part-skim mozzarella: 1 cup (4 oz) (115 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 400 °F / 200 °C. Mix the turkey, egg, breadcrumbs, Parmesan, onion, garlic, oregano and salt with your hands until just combined (overmixing makes tough meatballs). Roll into 24 meatballs, about 1.5 in / 4 cm each.
  2. Stir the marinara, broth and orzo together in a 9x13 in / 23x33 cm baking dish. Stir in the spinach. Nestle the meatballs on top.
  3. Cover tightly with foil. Bake 30 minutes.
  4. Uncover, stir the orzo gently (it can stick at the edges), and scatter the mozzarella on top. Bake 10-12 more minutes until the cheese is melted with browned spots, the orzo is tender with no hard center, and a meatball cut open reads 165 °F / 74 °C and shows no pink.
  5. Rest 10 minutes; the orzo soaks up the last of the sauce. Cut into 6 portions (4 meatballs each).
  6. Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  7. Portion and freeze: divide into 6 labeled containers (date on the lid), leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace, and freeze flat. Freeze in single-portion containers; the orzo is softer after thawing, which is normal.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven for 40-45 min at 400 °F / 200 °C. Slot it with the 425 °F sheet-pan proteins; the stovetop stays free for soups.

Fix it

  • Orzo crunchy in the middle: the dish was not covered tight or the oven runs cool. Add 1/4 cup hot water, cover again, and bake 8 more minutes.
  • Dry meatballs: you used too much breadcrumb or mixed too long. Keep the 1.5 lb turkey to 1/3 cup crumbs and mix just until combined.

Swap

Swap the orzo for the same weight of any small pasta, or for brown rice (needs 50 minutes covered). Dairy-free: skip the Parmesan and mozzarella and add 1/2 tsp salt.

Freezer meal

FM9

Black Bean & Sweet Potato Burritos

Makes 6 burritos (1 per portion, ~350 g each)Active 30 minTotal 55 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
530kcal
24 gprotein
70 gcarbs
18 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.85
Allergenswheat, soy, milk
Fridge3 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Microwave unwrapped, wrapped in a damp paper towel, 1.5-2 min, flipping once; or bake at 375 °F / 190 °C for 20 min. The center must reach 165 °F / 74 °C. Finish in a dry skillet 1 min per side for a crisp outside.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: S1 Chipotle-Lime Yogurt, S2 Tomatillo Salsa Verde, V6 Chopped Cucumber-Tomato Salad

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • sweet potatoes, peeled and cut in 1/2 in cubes: 1.3 lb (600 g)
  • olive oil: 1 tbsp (14 g)
  • firm tofu, crumbled: 1 block (16 oz) (454 g)
  • chili powder: 1 tbsp (8 g)
  • ground cumin: 2 tsp (5 g)
  • garlic powder: 1 tsp (3 g)
  • kosher salt: 1 tsp (5 g)
  • red bell pepper, diced: 1 (150 g)
  • black beans, canned, drained and rinsed: 2 cans (15 oz) (480 g)
  • shredded Mexican-blend cheese: 1 cup (4 oz) (115 g)
  • large flour tortillas (10 in): 6 (390 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C. Toss the sweet potato and pepper with the oil, 1 tsp of the chili powder and half the salt on a sheet pan. Roast 20-25 minutes, stirring once, until a fork goes in easily and the edges are browned.
  2. While they roast, press the tofu between paper towels for 5 minutes. Crumble it into a nonstick skillet over medium-high heat with the rest of the chili powder, cumin, garlic powder and salt. Cook 6-8 minutes until it looks dry, golden and crumbly like scrambled egg.
  3. Mash half the black beans lightly with a fork and leave the rest whole. Mix beans, tofu and roasted vegetables in a big bowl. Taste and add salt if needed. Let the filling cool 10 minutes (hot filling makes the tortilla soggy and steams in the freezer).
  4. Warm the tortillas 15 seconds each in the microwave so they fold without cracking. Put about 1 cup filling and 2.5 tbsp cheese in the lower third of each tortilla. Fold in the sides, then roll up tightly.
  5. Cool completely at room temperature, no longer than 2 hours total. Wrap each burrito in parchment, then foil or a freezer bag. Press out the air.
  6. Freeze flat on a tray for 1 hour, then stack. Label with the date.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven for 25 minutes at 425 °F / 220 °C, the same heat as the sheet-pan proteins. Rolling happens at the end, off the stove, while the oven items rest.

Fix it

  • Burritos burst open: you overfilled them. Use about 1 cup of filling, and fold in the sides before rolling.
  • Soggy after reheating: the filling was warm when rolled, or they sat wrapped warm. Cool them fully, and finish in a dry skillet.

Swap

Swap the tofu for 2 cups of cooked shredded chicken (P2 or P3) for a meat version, still 165 °F / 74 °C. Dairy-free: skip the cheese. Gluten-free: use large corn or gluten-free tortillas and warm them well.

Freezer meal

FM10

Creamy Chicken & Broccoli Pasta Bake

Makes 6 portions (~335 g each)Active 35 minTotal 60 minOven 375 °F / 190 °C
530kcal
45 gprotein
51 gcarbs
16 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.35
Allergenswheat, milk
Fridge3 days
Freezer2 months
ReheatThaw overnight in the fridge (never on the counter). Microwave covered 2-3 min with 1 tbsp milk drizzled on top, stirring once; or bake covered at 350 °F / 175 °C for 15-20 min, until 165 °F / 74 °C in the center.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: V4 Garlic Green Beans, V6 Chopped Cucumber-Tomato Salad, S12 Creamy Tomato-Garlic (a spoonful on the side)

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • boneless skinless chicken breasts, cut in 1 in cubes: 1.5 lb (700 g)
  • dry short pasta (penne or rotini): 10 oz (285 g)
  • broccoli florets, small: 4 cups (350 g)
  • butter: 2 tbsp (28 g)
  • all-purpose flour: 3 tbsp (25 g)
  • garlic, minced: 3 cloves (9 g)
  • 1% milk: 2.5 cups (620 g)
  • light cream cheese: 3 oz (6 tbsp) (85 g)
  • grated Parmesan: 1/2 cup (2 oz) (50 g)
  • kosher salt: 1 tsp (5 g)
  • black pepper: 1/2 tsp (1 g)
  • shredded part-skim mozzarella: 3/4 cup (3 oz) (85 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 375 °F / 190 °C. Boil the pasta in salted water 3 minutes less than the package says (it finishes in the oven). Add the broccoli for the last 2 minutes. Drain; the pasta should be chewy in the center.
  2. In a large skillet, cook the chicken in a little of the butter over medium-high heat 5-6 minutes until white inside and 165 °F / 74 °C. Move it to the pasta bowl.
  3. In the same skillet melt the rest of the butter over medium. Whisk in the flour and garlic and cook 1 minute. Pour in the milk slowly, whisking, and cook 4-5 minutes until the sauce coats the back of a spoon and a finger drawn across it leaves a clean line.
  4. Off the heat, whisk in the cream cheese until smooth, then the Parmesan, salt and pepper.
  5. Toss the pasta, broccoli, chicken and sauce together and spread into a 9x13 in / 23x33 cm dish. Sprinkle with the mozzarella. Bake uncovered 20-25 minutes until bubbling at the edges and golden in spots; the center must read 165 °F / 74 °C.
  6. Rest 10 minutes, cut into 6 squares. Cool fast: within 2 hours of cooking, ladle into shallow containers no deeper than 2 in / 5 cm. Leave uncovered about 20 minutes (or set the containers in an ice bath), then lid and refrigerate once the steam stops.
  7. Portion and freeze: divide into 6 labeled containers (date on the lid), leave 1 in / 2.5 cm of headspace, and freeze flat. Wrap squares individually or freeze in flat containers.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven 20-25 min at 375 °F / 190 °C, with the pasta water and sauce on the stove first (minutes 0-30).

Fix it

  • Lumpy sauce: the milk went in too fast. Whisk hard off the heat, or push the sauce through a sieve.
  • Dry and thick after freezing: stir in 1 tbsp milk per portion when reheating. Cream sauces thicken in the freezer.

Swap

Swap the pasta for the same weight of dry whole-wheat pasta or chickpea pasta for more protein. Swap the chicken for 2 cups of canned white beans (vegetarian). Gluten-free: use gluten-free pasta and 3 tbsp cornstarch (instead of flour).

Chapter 19

High-Protein Snacks

Eight snack boxes for between meals. Half need no cooking at all (SN1, SN2, SN6, SN7). Each one shows its protein, so you know what it adds to the day.

How to use this block
  • Make them with the rest of the Sunday session, in the cold-prep gaps.
  • Most don't freeze. The peanut butter bites (SN4) and roasted chickpeas (SN5) do.
  • Keep them cold: at 40 °F / 4 °C or below, and no more than 2 hours out of the fridge (FSIS).
  • Peanuts and tree nuts show up in this block (SN1, SN4, SN8). Check the column if you pack snacks for other people.

Snack

SN1

Greek Yogurt Protein Cups (3 flavors)

Makes 5 portions (200 g yogurt + toppings; nutrition is the average of the 5 cups)Active 10 minTotal 10 min
280kcal
25 gprotein
26 gcarbs
10 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $2.30
Allergensmilk, peanuts, tree nuts
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (see Reheat)
ReheatEat cold. Do not freeze. Keep at 40 °F / 4 °C or colder; do not leave out more than 2 hours.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: D3 Greek Yogurt Overnight Oats (same yogurt tub), D4 Peanut Butter-Banana Overnight Oats; stands alone as a snack

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • plain nonfat Greek yogurt: 4 cups (1000 g)
  • strawberries, diced (Berry-Vanilla cups): 1 cup (150 g)
  • honey (all flavors): 3 tbsp (63 g)
  • vanilla extract (Berry-Vanilla): 1 tsp (4 g)
  • unsweetened cocoa powder (Cocoa-Peanut cups): 2 tbsp (11 g)
  • smooth peanut butter (Cocoa-Peanut cups): 4 tbsp (64 g)
  • ground cinnamon (Cinnamon-Walnut cup): 1/2 tsp (1 g)
  • chopped walnuts (Cinnamon-Walnut cup): 2 tbsp (15 g)
  • chia seeds (Berry-Vanilla cups): 2 tsp (10 g)

Steps

  1. Set out 5 jars or containers (about 1 1/4 cups / 300 ml each) with lids. Put 200 g (a heaping 3/4 cup) of yogurt in each. Weighing on a scale is the fastest way to get even cups.
  2. Berry-Vanilla (2 cups): stir 2 tsp honey and 1/2 tsp vanilla into the yogurt of each cup until smooth. Top each with 75 g (about 1/2 cup) diced strawberries and 1 tsp chia seeds. The strawberries go on top, not stirred in, so the cup does not turn watery.
  3. Cocoa-Peanut (2 cups): stir 1 tbsp cocoa and 1 tsp honey into the yogurt of each cup until there are no streaks. Spoon 2 tbsp peanut butter on top (warm it 10 seconds in the microwave so it spreads).
  4. Cinnamon-Walnut (1 cup): stir 1 tbsp honey and the cinnamon into the yogurt. Top with the walnuts.
  5. Done when each cup is smooth and thick like pudding with no dry cocoa clumps. Put on the lids and refrigerate right away.
  6. Do not freeze: the yogurt separates and turns grainy. Add crunchy toppings (walnuts, granola) just before eating if you want them to stay crisp.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. Assemble in the last 10 minutes of the session, while the oven and stove items rest.

Fix it

  • Watery cups: the yogurt wasn't strained. Pour off the liquid on top and stir; next time buy a thick Greek yogurt or strain 10 minutes in a sieve.
  • Cocoa clumps: mix the cocoa with the honey first into a paste, then into the yogurt.

Swap

Dairy-free: use unsweetened soy yogurt (protein will be lower, about 8-10 g per cup). Nut-free: swap the peanut butter for sunflower seed butter and the walnuts for pumpkin seeds.

SN2

Hummus & Veg Boxes

Makes 5 portions (60 g hummus + 30 g cheese + 190 g raw veg)Active 15 minTotal 15 min
270kcal
14 gprotein
22 gcarbs
16 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.65
Allergenssesame, milk
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (see Reheat)
ReheatEat cold. Not freezer-friendly (the vegetables turn limp).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: B6 Wraps & Tortillas, S8 Lemon-Tahini (use instead of hummus); stands alone as a snack

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • hummus, store-bought: 1 1/4 cups (300 g)
  • part-skim mozzarella, cut in cubes (or string cheese): 5 oz (150 g)
  • cucumber, cut in sticks: 1 large (400 g)
  • carrots, cut in sticks: 4 medium (250 g)
  • red bell pepper, cut in strips: 2 medium (300 g)
  • everything bagel seasoning or paprika (pinch per box): 1 tsp (3 g)

Steps

  1. Wash and dry all the vegetables completely (wet veg makes the box soggy and shortens its life). Cut cucumber, carrot and pepper into sticks about 3 in / 8 cm long.
  2. Spoon 60 g (about 1/4 cup) of hummus into the small compartment of each of 5 containers, or into small lidded cups. Sprinkle a pinch of paprika on top.
  3. Divide the cheese cubes (30 g each) and the vegetables (about 190 g each) between the boxes. Wrap the cheese in parchment if you want it fully dry.
  4. Done when every box has hummus, cheese and three colors of veg with nothing touching the hummus except the dip cup. Lid and refrigerate.
  5. Eat within 4 days. Keep the boxes cold (40 °F / 4 °C or below), especially in a lunch bag; use an ice pack if it will sit more than 2 hours.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 15 minutes, any time; it uses no oven or burner.

Fix it

  • Soggy sticks: dry the vegetables with a towel and store the hummus in its own cup.
  • Cucumber turns watery by day 4: salt nothing in advance, and swap cucumber for celery after day 3.

Swap

Dairy-free: swap the mozzarella for 1/2 cup of roasted chickpeas (SN5) per box. Sesame-free: use a hummus without tahini or mashed white beans with lemon and garlic.

Snack

SN3

Perfect Hard-Boiled Eggs

Makes 5 portions (2 large eggs (about 100 g peeled))Active 5 minTotal 25 min
160kcal
13 gprotein
1 gcarbs
11 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.40
Allergensegg
Fridge7 days
Freezernot recommended (see Reheat)
ReheatEat cold. Do not microwave in the shell (they can burst). Do not freeze: the whites turn rubbery.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: D1 Spinach-Feta Egg Bites (same protein, different style), V5 Sesame Cabbage-Carrot Slaw; stands alone as a snack

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • large eggs, cold from the fridge: 10 (500 g)
  • kosher salt (pinch per portion): 1/2 tsp (3 g)
  • smoked paprika or everything seasoning (optional, pinch per portion): 1/2 tsp (1 g)

Steps

  1. Put the eggs in a single layer in a large pot. Cover with cold water by 1 in / 2.5 cm. Bring to a full rolling boil over high heat.
  2. As soon as the water boils, cover the pot, turn off the heat, and slide it off the burner. Set a timer for 12 minutes.
  3. While they sit, fill a big bowl with ice and water. When the timer rings, move the eggs into the ice bath for 10 minutes. Cold shock stops the cooking and makes them easy to peel.
  4. Peel under a trickle of cool water, starting at the wide end where the air pocket is. A done egg has a fully set, pale yellow yolk with no green ring and no wet center.
  5. Keep the eggs in their shells in a lidded container for up to 7 days; peeled eggs keep best in a container with a damp paper towel and are best within 5 days. Divide into 5 portions of 2 eggs. Add the salt and paprika when you eat them.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop, minute 0. It is 12 minutes of waiting, so start it first and forget it.

Fix it

  • Green-gray ring around the yolk: they were overcooked. Stick to 12 minutes off heat and go straight to the ice bath.
  • Shells stick: your eggs were very fresh. Use eggs 1-2 weeks old, and peel under water.

Swap

Make them deviled-style: halve the eggs, mash the yolks with 1 tbsp plain Greek yogurt and 1 tsp mustard per 4 yolks, and spoon them back (eat within 3 days). Want fewer calories: eat 2 whole eggs plus 2 extra whites boiled the same way.

Snack

SN4

Peanut Butter Oat Bites

Makes 5 portions (3 bites (about 70 g))Active 15 minTotal 45 min
330kcal
11 gprotein
38 gcarbs
17 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.60
Allergenspeanuts
Fridge7 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatEat cold or at room temperature. To freeze, set the bites on a tray for 1 hour, then bag them. Thaw 15 minutes on the counter or overnight in the fridge. (No raw egg or meat, so counter thawing is safe here.)
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: D4 Peanut Butter-Banana Overnight Oats; stands alone as a snack

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • old-fashioned rolled oats: 1 1/4 cups (120 g)
  • smooth peanut butter: 1/2 cup (128 g)
  • honey: 1/4 cup (85 g)
  • ground flaxseed: 2 tbsp (20 g)
  • unsweetened cocoa powder: 1 tbsp (8 g)
  • vanilla extract: 1 tsp (4 g)
  • kosher salt: 1/4 tsp (1 g)

Steps

  1. Line a tray or plate with parchment. Mix the oats, flax, cocoa and salt in a bowl.
  2. Warm the peanut butter and honey together in the microwave for 20 seconds (or in a small pot on low for 1 minute). Stir in the vanilla.
  3. Pour the wet mix over the oats and stir until no dry oats remain. It should hold together when you squeeze a spoonful. If it is crumbly, add 1 tsp water; if it is sticky, add 1 tbsp oats.
  4. Chill the bowl 15 minutes so the mix firms up and is easy to roll.
  5. Roll into 15 balls, about 1 tbsp (25 g) each, and put them on the tray. Done when each ball is firm and holds its shape without leaving a greasy mark on your palm.
  6. Chill 30 minutes more, then store in an airtight container. Divide into 5 portions of 3.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold, no cooking. 15 minutes of work. Do it while an oven item bakes and finish the chilling in the fridge.

Fix it

  • Too crumbly: add 1 tsp water or 1 tsp honey, mix, and test again.
  • Too sticky to roll: chill the bowl 15 more minutes, or wet your hands lightly.

Swap

Peanut-free: use sunflower seed butter (same amount). Lower sugar: use 3 tbsp honey and add 1 tbsp more oats.

Snack

SN5

Crunchy Roasted Chickpeas

Makes 5 portions (about 100 g roasted (3/4 cup))Active 10 minTotal 50 minOven 425 °F / 220 °C
230kcal
10 gprotein
34 gcarbs
7 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $0.50
Allergensnone
Fridge5 days
Freezer3 months
ReheatRe-crisp in a 400 °F / 200 °C oven or air fryer for 5 minutes. Counter: 2 days in a loosely closed jar. Fridge: 5 days, but they soften. Freezer: 3 months in a bag; re-crisp from frozen.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: P10 Smoky Spiced Chickpeas (a bigger, saucier version), V6 Chopped Cucumber-Tomato Salad (as a crunchy topper); stands alone as a snack

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • canned chickpeas, drained and rinsed: 3 cans (15 oz) (720 g)
  • olive oil: 1 tbsp (14 g)
  • smoked paprika: 2 tsp (5 g)
  • garlic powder: 1 tsp (3 g)
  • ground cumin: 1 tsp (2 g)
  • kosher salt: 3/4 tsp (4 g)

Steps

  1. Heat the oven to 425 °F / 220 °C. Dry the chickpeas very well: roll them in a clean towel and rub off the loose skins. Dry chickpeas turn crisp; wet ones steam and stay soft.
  2. Spread them on two sheet pans (don't crowd them). Toss with the oil until lightly glossy.
  3. Roast 25-30 minutes, shaking the pans every 10 minutes. They are done when they rattle on the pan, look deep golden, and one cooled chickpea crunches when bitten.
  4. Right out of the oven, toss with the paprika, garlic powder, cumin and salt so the spices stick to the hot oil.
  5. Cool fully on the pans (15 minutes) before storing, or steam in the container will make them soft. Divide into 5 portions of about 3/4 cup / 100 g.
  6. Store in a container that is not fully sealed for the first day, then close it. They are crispest the first 2 days.

Sunday-sheet slot

Oven at 425 °F / 220 °C for 25-30 min on the second rack. Same heat as the sheet-pan proteins, so they ride along.

Fix it

  • Chewy, not crisp: not dry enough or the pan was crowded. Dry them again, use two pans, and roast 8 minutes longer.
  • Burnt on the outside, soft inside: the oven runs hot. Drop to 400 °F / 200 °C and roast 5 minutes longer.

Swap

Sweet version: skip the paprika, garlic and cumin; toss with 1 tsp cinnamon and 1 tbsp honey after roasting. Swap the chickpeas for canned white beans (they roast softer).

Snack

SN6

Turkey & Cheese Roll-Ups

Makes 5 portions (3 roll-ups (about 135 g))Active 15 minTotal 15 min
210kcal
22 gprotein
4 gcarbs
11 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.90
Allergensmilk
Fridge3 days
Freezernot recommended (see Reheat)
ReheatEat cold. Do not freeze (cheese and cucumber go grainy and watery).
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: B6 Wraps & Tortillas (roll a few inside a wrap for a lunch), V6 Chopped Cucumber-Tomato Salad; stands alone as a snack

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • low-sodium deli turkey breast slices: 10 oz (about 15 slices) (300 g)
  • Swiss cheese slices: 5 oz (15 slices) (150 g)
  • light cream cheese: 3 tbsp (45 g)
  • cucumber, cut in thin sticks: 1 medium (150 g)

Steps

  1. Lay out 15 slices of turkey on a clean board. Spread a thin layer of cream cheese (about 1 tsp) over each slice, leaving a 1/2 in / 1 cm border.
  2. Top each with a slice of Swiss and a cucumber stick laid across one end.
  3. Roll up tightly from the cucumber end. Done when the roll holds closed on its own without tearing the turkey.
  4. Pack 3 roll-ups per container, seam side down, with a sheet of parchment between layers so they don't stick.
  5. Keep cold at 40 °F / 4 °C or below and eat within 3 days. Deli meat is a higher-risk food; if you are pregnant or have a weak immune system, heat deli meat to steaming (165 °F / 74 °C) before eating, or choose another snack.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 15 minutes at the end of the session. Make it the same day you shop, since deli meat is best fresh.

Fix it

  • Rolls come undone: the cream cheese layer was too thin or the slices too cold and stiff. Let them sit out 5 minutes and spread to the edge.
  • Cucumber leaks water: pat the sticks dry on paper towel, or use bell pepper strips instead.

Swap

Swap the Swiss for provolone or part-skim mozzarella slices. Dairy-free: skip the cheese and use a smear of hummus. Swap the turkey for roast chicken breast deli slices.

Snack

SN7

Cottage Cheese Ranch Dip

Makes 5 portions (1/2 cup dip (120 g) + about 150 g raw veg)Active 10 minTotal 10 min
130kcal
13 gprotein
14 gcarbs
2 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.40
Allergensmilk
Fridge5 days
Freezernot recommended (see Reheat)
ReheatEat cold. Do not freeze: it splits and turns grainy.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: V5 Sesame Cabbage-Carrot Slaw, B6 Wraps & Tortillas (as a spread), P3 Never-Dry Baked Chicken Breast (as a dip for sliced chicken); stands alone as a snack

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • 2% cottage cheese: 2 cups (450 g)
  • plain nonfat Greek yogurt: 1/2 cup (125 g)
  • lemon juice: 1 tbsp (15 g)
  • garlic powder: 1/2 tsp (2 g)
  • onion powder: 1/2 tsp (2 g)
  • dried dill: 1 tsp (1 g)
  • fresh parsley, finely chopped: 2 tbsp (8 g)
  • kosher salt: 1/2 tsp (3 g)
  • baby carrots: 10 oz (300 g)
  • celery sticks: 5 stalks (250 g)
  • cucumber, in rounds: 1 large (200 g)

Steps

  1. Put the cottage cheese, yogurt, lemon juice, garlic powder, onion powder and dill in a blender or food processor.
  2. Blend 45-60 seconds, scraping down once, until completely smooth and pourable like thick sour cream, with no visible curds.
  3. Stir in the parsley and salt by hand. Taste: it should be tangy and savory. Add a pinch more salt or a few drops of lemon if needed.
  4. Spoon 1/2 cup (120 g) of dip into each of 5 small lidded containers or jars. Chill at least 1 hour; it thickens as it chills.
  5. Cut the veg (carrots, celery, cucumber) and divide into the 5 boxes, about 150 g each. Keep the dip and the veg in separate compartments.
  6. Eat within 5 days. Stir before eating since a little liquid may rise to the top.

Sunday-sheet slot

Cold. 10 minutes, any time. A blender and nothing else.

Fix it

  • Dip too thin: strain the cottage cheese 10 minutes before blending, or stir in 2 tbsp more yogurt.
  • Tastes bland: it needs salt and acid. Add salt in pinches and a squeeze of lemon, then rest it 30 minutes so the flavor develops.

Swap

Lactose-light: use lactose-free cottage cheese. Spicy: add 1 tsp hot sauce. Swap the veg for whole-wheat pita triangles or the baked chickpeas from SN5.

Snack

SN8

Edamame Snack Boxes

Makes 5 portions (about 90 g shelled edamame + 90 g veg + 14 g almonds)Active 10 minTotal 20 min
220kcal
15 gprotein
15 gcarbs
13 gfat
PER PORTION · USDA FoodData Central
Cost / portionabout $1.65
Allergenssoy, tree nuts, sesame, wheat
Fridge4 days
Freezernot recommended (see Reheat)
ReheatEat cold, or warm the edamame alone 30 seconds in the microwave. The assembled box is not freezer-friendly (vegetables go limp); plain cooked edamame freezes for 3 months.
Cost: US average retail prices, USDA ERS (meat, eggs, dairy: Sep 2026; fruit and vegetables: 2023 data); your store will differ.

Goes with: V5 Sesame Cabbage-Carrot Slaw, A2 and A7 plates (a spoonful on top); stands alone as a snack

Ingredients (US + metric)

  • frozen shelled edamame: 1 bag (16 oz) (454 g)
  • cherry tomatoes, halved: 2 cups (300 g)
  • red bell pepper, cut in strips: 1 medium (150 g)
  • dry-roasted almonds: 1/4 cup (70 g)
  • toasted sesame oil: 1 tsp (5 g)
  • low-sodium soy sauce: 2 tsp (12 g)
  • flaky sea salt (pinch per box): 1/4 tsp (1 g)

Steps

  1. Bring a pot of water to a boil. Add the frozen edamame and cook 4-5 minutes until they are bright green and tender with a little bite, and taste fully warm all the way through.
  2. Drain and cool in a colander under cold water for 1 minute. Shake dry and pat with a towel. Wet edamame turn the box soggy.
  3. Toss the edamame with the sesame oil, soy sauce and half the salt in a bowl.
  4. Divide into 5 containers (about 90 g edamame each). Add the tomato halves and pepper strips (about 90 g of veg per box), and pack the almonds (14 g) in a small cup or corner so they stay crunchy.
  5. Done when the edamame look glossy, not wet. Lid and chill right away. Eat within 4 days. Keep at 40 °F / 4 °C or colder.

Sunday-sheet slot

Stovetop, 5 minutes of active time. Boil the edamame while the oven items rest.

Fix it

  • Edamame tough and bland: they cooked too little or are old. Cook 2 minutes longer, and salt while they're still warm.
  • Watery box: dry the edamame well and put the tomato in a separate cup if you pack it more than 2 days ahead.

Swap

Soy-free is not possible with edamame; swap for 1 1/2 cups of roasted chickpeas (SN5) per 5 boxes. Nut-free: swap the almonds for pumpkin seeds. Gluten-free: use tamari.

Chapter 20

Storage Chart · All 60 Recipes

"is it fine to store my food like this for four-five days?"

— thread title, meal prep forum

One table to check in 5 seconds: how long it keeps in the fridge, how long in the freezer, and how to bring it back. Print it and tape it inside a cupboard door (it's in the printable pack too).

The rules behind the numbers

All from USDA Food Safety and Inspection Service (FSIS):

  1. Fridge at 40 °F (4 °C) or colder. Cooked leftovers keep 3-4 days. Count from the day you cook (day 0).
  2. The day-5 rule (this book): anything you'll eat on day 5 or later goes in the freezer on cooking day. Move it to the fridge the morning before.
  3. 2 hours max at room temperature after cooking, 1 hour if the room is above 90 °F (32 °C). Spread food shallow so it cools fast.
  4. Freezer at 0 °F (-18 °C). Frozen food stays safe as long as it stays frozen. The months below are for quality (taste and texture), not safety.
  5. Thaw in the fridge, never on the counter. Or on defrost in the microwave, then reheat and eat right away.
  6. Reheat leftovers to 165 °F (74 °C). Cover, stir halfway, check the thickest piece.
  7. When in doubt, throw it out. If it smells off or you don't know how old it is.

"Freezer: no (texture)" means the recipe is safe frozen but comes out watery, grainy or limp. Eat it from the fridge.

The chart

Microwave covered 3-4 min, stirring halfway.

Chapter 21

Macro, Cost & Allergen Index

"Presenting the macros is a great plus."

— reader review

Looking for the plate with the most protein? One without milk? This index lists all 48 signature plates, sorted by protein, highest first. Then it lists which plates are free of each allergen.

How the numbers were made

  • One box = one standard portion of each of the 4 parts (protein, base, vegetable, sauce), as on the recipe cards.
  • Calculated from USDA FoodData Central SR Legacy with the gram weight of every ingredient, sauce included. Kcal rounded to 10, protein, carbs and fat to 1 g.
  • Your boxes in the plan weeks can differ, because some batches are shared by more or fewer boxes. The week chapters show those numbers.
  • These numbers describe the food. They are not advice on how much to eat. For that, talk to a registered dietitian or your doctor.

At a glance: plates run from 510 to 850 kcal. 42 of 48 have 40 g of protein or more. The 6 under 40 g are the beef-and-lentil plates (M4, D-6, C5) and the chickpea plates (M7, D-2, D-9). Their real number is shown, not rounded up.
